- This is a REVIEW-ONLY task. Do NOT edit, rewrite, or "fix" any file under experiments/k1709/. Do not create new files there. You may run read-only commands (python -c, grep, pytest) to verify claims, and you may write scratch files under /tmp only. Your deliverable is the review text on stdout.

YOUR TASK — adjudicate, do not rubber-stamp.

The previous audit (codex_review_rev1_20260714.txt) listed blocking defects. The authors claim they fixed them. Do NOT take the authors' self-report at face value. For EACH item below, independently determine whether it is genuinely FIXED, PARTIAL, or NOT FIXED, and say so explicitly with file:line evidence.

(A) C6 — cell-key collision (the most serious prior defect).
    Previously, `LOSS_CACHE[cell]` / `by_cell` keys were last-write-wins: robustness-branch cells overwrote primary cells. Consequence: 4 of 10 primary exclusion p-values were silently replaced by robustness-cell values, and `primary_cells` actually contained 54 entries instead of 10. The authors say the cell key now carries a family/proxy/flow-lag namespace and `primary_cells` is back to 10.
    You must verify:
      - Is the cell key now genuinely unique across ALL code paths that write it? Enumerate the write sites.
      - Is there an actual uniqueness TEST (not just a comment)? If a test exists, does it really catch a collision, or would it also pass under the old last-write-wins code?
      - Given the current keying, are the reported exclusion p-values and the simultaneous (multiplicity-adjusted) bounds now trustworthy? Cross-check against k1709_results.json.

(B) End-to-end runnability / key drift.
    A prior rename `size_at_beta_0` -> `false_positive_rate_at_beta_0` left dangling references. Verify the rename is complete EVERYWHERE, including figure-generation code and render_readme.py, and that the main program can actually run end to end. State exactly how you checked (grep for the old key; static trace of the figure path; or an actual run).

(C) README claim surface.
    Earlier drafts asserted a hard `NULL` result and claimed the ability to exclude an effect as large as `+16.2% RV`. Both were RETRACTED; the verdict is now `INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`.
    Check whether README.md (and any other reader-facing surface, incl. the JSON's summary/verdict fields) still asserts either retracted claim as a CONCLUSION. Note carefully: mentioning them inside an explicit retraction narrative ("we previously claimed X; we withdraw it because Y") is LEGITIMATE and must NOT be flagged. Only flag it if a retracted claim is asserted as a live finding.

(D) The six wording/scope residuals (R1-R6) from the prior audit. Confirm each is genuinely resolved, not cosmetically reworded:
    R1. Power over-claiming (asserting "we can rule out effects of size X" when the design cannot support it).
    R2. `_first_at` reported as a point estimate when it is only a coarse grid point (a detection threshold on a discrete grid, not an estimated MDE).
    R3. beta=0 rejection rate mislabeled as "size" (it is a false-positive rate under a specific DGP, not test size in the exact sense).
    R4. Fixed-window raw DM mislabeled as "nested-biased" (nesting bias is a rolling/estimated-parameter issue; under a fixed window with pre-estimated parameters the DM statistic is not subject to that particular bias — verify the authors' handling is now correct AND correctly described).
    R5. "bounded-null" language (claiming a bounded null when the design yields no such bound).
    R6. `unexpected_z` expanding window (does the standardization use only past information? any look-ahead in the expanding-window mean/sd of the flow surprise?).

(E) INDEPENDENT CHALLENGE — do NOT defer to the prior audit here.
    The central inferential move is a fixed-window Giacomini-White (GW) conditional predictive-ability test. The prior audit accepted the authors' claim that this is a valid FIXED-scheme GW test.
    You must decide this yourself, from the code, not from the prior verdict:
      - Is the "fixed-window GW" implementation merely a Diebold-Mariano test in disguise, i.e. a plain unconditional DM test relabeled as GW? A genuine GW test conditions on an instrument/test-function h_t and tests E[h_t * dL_{t+1}] = 0 with a chi-square statistic of dimension dim(h); DM is the special case h_t = 1. If the code only ever uses h_t = 1, calling it GW is a mislabel.
      - Are the model parameters genuinely FIXED (estimated once on an initial sample and never re-estimated), or re-estimated in a rolling/recursive scheme (in which case the fixed-scheme asymptotics invoked are invalid and inference needs a nesting/parameter-estimation correction)?
      - Is the HAC/Newey-West lag choice and the variance estimator appropriate for the loss-differential series?
    Report your independent finding even if it contradicts the prior audit.

(F) LOOK-AHEAD BIAS SWEEP (highest-risk class in this project).
    Independently trace: is every predictor lagged relative to the target? Look for `.shift(1)` or equivalent on flows, on the RV target construction, on any standardization/normalization (full-sample means/sds are a classic leak), on threshold/breakpoint selection, and on train/test splits. Also verify that all stochastic procedures (bootstrap, Monte Carlo, simulation, sampling, splits) are seeded.

HONESTY LINE — read this carefully before deciding PASS/FAIL:
- The experiment's verdict is `INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`. An inconclusive / null result is a PERFECTLY ACCEPTABLE research outcome in this project; honest reporting of nulls is mandated. You are judging whether the METHODOLOGY AND THE CLAIM SURFACE ARE TRUSTWORTHY — NOT whether an effect was found.
- Therefore: do NOT fail the experiment merely because the result is inconclusive or the power is low, PROVIDED the write-up says so honestly and does not over-claim.
- Equally: do NOT pass it to be agreeable. If a blocking defect survives (silent key collision, look-ahead, invalid inference, a retracted claim still asserted as a finding, code that cannot run), say FAIL and list it.
- PASS means: the code does what it says, the numbers in the results/README are reproducible from the code, the inference is validly specified and correctly labeled, and the claim surface does not exceed the evidence.

313:### 6.2 Power test — 管線能不能找到真的存在的訊號？
315:`test_power_injected_signal_is_detected`：**植入**一條真正驅動次日 RV 的合成 flow（log RV_{t+1} = 持續性基底 + 1.2·|z_t|），要求管線把它找回來。
321:**但這只是「管線通了」，不是「設計有功效」**（獨立審查的正確批評）：這個植入訊號解釋了 70% 的變異、且 target 無雜訊。真正的功效問題由 §5.0 的 **MDE 曲線**在**真實面板**上回答——那才是決定本 null 能說什麼的東西。兩者缺一不可：power test 排除**程式錯誤**，MDE 曲線界定**宣稱範圍**。
323:### 6.3 Placebo test — 管線會不會無中生有？
325:`test_placebo_scrambled_flow_is_not_detected`：同一套機器餵入純雜訊 flow → 要求 **|DM t| < 3**。通過。
334:- **突變測試**：把 AR(5) 的 lag 向量反轉 → `test_unexpected_flow_ar5_lag_ordering` **確實失敗**（測試有牙齒，不是裝飾）。
337:- **測試套件**：**26 passed**（解析陷阱 / z-score 嚴格前向 / AR(5) lag 序 / 日曆對齊 / **h=5 重疊目標訓練過濾** / **方向閘門反轉** / power / placebo）。
339:> **結論：這個管線可證明地「找得到真訊號」（power test）、「不會無中生有」（placebo test）、且「評分方式不偏袒基準」（smearing test）。因此 NULL 是關於資料的陳述，不是關於程式的陳述。**

### C1 — FIXED

你的理論主張站得住。GW 原文明確要求估計窗口有有限上界、排除 expanding window，並明說無條件 GW 統計量與 DM 統計量重合；不同的是 forecasting-method asymptotic experiment，估計不確定性不會消失，因此可處理 nested methods。[Giacomini–White 原文](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdf/10.1111%2Fj.1468-0262.2006.00718.x)

rev1 確實不是只換函式名：共同 complete-case mask、共同 `start:end`、固定 250 筆、forward-label embargo 都真的落在 [k1709.py:858](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:858) 與 [k1709.py:909](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:909)。

但有兩個新瑕疵：

- fixed-window 那份「raw DM」不應再被描述成 `nested-biased`。它吃的就是同一條 GW-valid loss stream；只有 expanding diagnostic 才不具這個合法性。[k1709.py:1144](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1144)
- 兩個統計量並非數值「完全相同」。GW lag covariance 除以 `n`，helper DM 除以 `n-lag`；例如 BTC H1 是 `1.298815` 對 `1.299319`。[results:866](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709_results.json:866)

另有一個非 primary 問題：`unexpected_z` 的 AR(5) 實際用全部歷史資料擴張估計，卻被列入 fixed-memory full-family GW；不影響十個 primary cells，但「所有 GW robustness 都 bounded-memory」不成立。[k1709.py:537](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:537)

修好的部分：

- power 不再承載 exclusion，+16.2% RV bound 在新程式中已撤回。

- 最新主程式無法跑完：power output 已改成 `false_positive_rate_at_beta_0`，但 figure code 仍讀 `size_at_beta_0`。[k1709.py:1521](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1521)、[k1709.py:2217](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:2217)

最新 source 把 exclusion 改成 intersection-union、每格用 raw p 且要求全部拒絕；這個邏輯正確。但 bounded-null 文字仍寫「Holm-adjusted」，renderer 也只展示 Holm p，報告口徑尚未同步。

- `LOSS_CACHE[cell]` 與 `by_cell={...}` 都是 last-write-wins。[k1709.py:1867](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1867)
- 因此 4/10 primary exclusion p 被 robustness cell 取代；例如 BTC h1 `.003054→.004806`、ETH h1 `.045727→.023967`。simultaneous bounds 也同樣被污染。
- `res["primary_cells"] = cells` 後仍繼續 append 同一 list，結果檔的 `primary_cells` 實際為 54 筆，與 `all_cells` 相同。[k1709.py:1732](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1732)

目前沒有一致的 rev1 artifact：

- results JSON 是 `INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`。[results:12629](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709_results.json:12629)
- README 仍是 v1，仍宣稱 `NULL` 與可排除 `+16.2% RV`。[README:3](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/README.md:3)
- renderer 對舊 results 會因 key drift 而 `KeyError`。[render_readme.py:348](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/render_readme.py:348)
- 現版單元測試是 52 passed，但沒有 main/render integration 或 cell-key uniqueness test。

所以：

- **可以支撐的統計內容**：十個 primary fixed-window GW cells 沒有提供穩健的增量預測證據。
- **不能認證目前整份 rev1**：source 無法端到端重跑，exclusion/bounds 被 key collision 污染，README 又仍在宣稱已撤回的結果。
- 修正並完整重跑後，若 primary GW 數值不變，合理 verdict 只能是 **`INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`**；不能是 bounded null，更不能恢復任何 RV-space exclusion。

- 最新主程式無法跑完：power output 已改成 `false_positive_rate_at_beta_0`，但 figure code 仍讀 `size_at_beta_0`。[k1709.py:1521](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1521)、[k1709.py:2217](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:2217)

最新 source 把 exclusion 改成 intersection-union、每格用 raw p 且要求全部拒絕；這個邏輯正確。但 bounded-null 文字仍寫「Holm-adjusted」，renderer 也只展示 Holm p，報告口徑尚未同步。

### C3 — FIXED

XNYS filter 確實在 flow feature／rolling scaler 之前執行。[k1709.py:205](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:205)、[k1709.py:351](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:351)

`state_lag`、`flow_lag` 已分別 shift，實際 `har_d`、`z` 來源值也有測試，不只驗 synthetic date metadata。[k1709.py:669](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:669)、[test_k1709.py:325](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/test_k1709.py:325)

### C6 — PARTIAL

raw p 保留到 Holm、smearing runs 進 registry，原修法本身成立。

但新 registry 流程引入了阻斷性碰撞：

- `cell` 只含 asset/horizon/alt，不含 family、proxy、flow lag、threshold 或 train window。[k1709.py:1240](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1240)
- `LOSS_CACHE[cell]` 與 `by_cell={...}` 都是 last-write-wins。[k1709.py:1867](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1867)
- 因此 4/10 primary exclusion p 被 robustness cell 取代；例如 BTC h1 `.003054→.004806`、ETH h1 `.045727→.023967`。simultaneous bounds 也同樣被污染。
- `res["primary_cells"] = cells` 後仍繼續 append 同一 list，結果檔的 `primary_cells` 實際為 54 筆，與 `all_cells` 相同。[k1709.py:1732](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1732)

目前碰撞碰巧沒有翻轉 `INCONCLUSIVE`，但 claim-bearing exclusion 數字與 confidence bounds 不可相信。

### 最終判決

目前沒有一致的 rev1 artifact：

- results JSON 是 `INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`。[results:12629](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709_results.json:12629)
- README 仍是 v1，仍宣稱 `NULL` 與可排除 `+16.2% RV`。[README:3](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/README.md:3)
- renderer 對舊 results 會因 key drift 而 `KeyError`。[render_readme.py:348](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/render_readme.py:348)
- 現版單元測試是 52 passed，但沒有 main/render integration 或 cell-key uniqueness test。

所以：

- **可以支撐的統計內容**：十個 primary fixed-window GW cells 沒有提供穩健的增量預測證據。
- **不能認證目前整份 rev1**：source 無法端到端重跑，exclusion/bounds 被 key collision 污染，README 又仍在宣稱已撤回的結果。
- 修正並完整重跑後，若 primary GW 數值不變，合理 verdict 只能是 **`INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM`**；不能是 bounded null，更不能恢復任何 RV-space exclusion。

     1	"""K1709-rev — Spot BTC/ETH ETF net flow shocks and next-day realized volatility.
     2	
     3	Research question
     4	-----------------
     5	Do daily *net creation/redemption flows* of spot Bitcoin/Ethereum ETFs carry
     6	incremental predictive content for BTC/ETH realized volatility at t+1 and t+5,
     7	once the standard HAR-RV volatility dynamics are controlled for?
     8	
     9	Orthogonality declaration
    10	-------------------------
    11	This is NOT the "ETF-ization changed the trading clock / session structure"
    12	line of work. The treatment here is the *flow* itself (dollar creations minus
    13	redemptions), not the calendar/microstructure of ETF trading hours.
    14	
    15	Inference design (this is what v1 got wrong)
    16	--------------------------------------------
    17	`HAR+ctrl` and `HAR+ctrl+flow` are STRICTLY NESTED. What breaks an ordinary
    18	loss-difference statistic here is not nesting by itself: it is nesting COMBINED
    19	WITH AN ESTIMATION SCHEME THAT DRIVES ESTIMATION ERROR TO ZERO. Under an
    20	expanding (recursive) window the two methods' forecasts converge under the null,
    21	the loss differential degenerates, and the statistic tilts toward the smaller
    22	model. Under a FIXED ROLLING window the estimation error never vanishes, the
    23	loss differential keeps a non-degenerate variance, and the comparison is legal
    24	again -- that is precisely Giacomini-White's (2006) construction. v1 pushed an
    25	expanding-window QLIKE loss through a raw Diebold-Mariano statistic and then
    26	bolted on a Clark-West helper that actually scores *variance-level squared
    27	error* -- three different estimands fused into a single NULL gate. See
    28	`codex_review_20260714.md` and the K1701 lesson in `docs/error_log.md`
    29	(2026-07-13 05:47).
    30	
    31	  nested-dm: diagnostic-only  -- every raw Diebold-Mariano number in this file is
    32	  descriptive and is tagged `feeds_gate=False`. None of them reaches a verdict.
    33	  Two DIFFERENT reasons sit behind that one tag, and they must not be conflated:
    34	  the EXPANDING-window statistic is diagnostic because it is INVALID (degenerate
    35	  null); the FIXED-window statistic is diagnostic because the verdict is carried
    36	  by the pre-registered Giacomini-White object, NOT because it is invalid -- it
    37	  runs on the same GW-legal loss stream and agrees with the gate statistic to ~3
    38	  decimals.
    39	
    40	What the claim actually rests on:
    41	  * Giacomini-White (2006) equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test on Patton
    42	    QLIKE, computed from a PAIRED FIXED ROLLING WINDOW. GW keeps estimation
    43	    uncertainty in the limiting experiment, which is exactly what makes it legal
    44	    for nested *methods* where a vanishing-estimation-error scheme is not.
    45	    Bounded estimator memory is a condition on the whole FORECASTING METHOD, not
    46	    just on the final regression: the one robustness cell whose regressor is
    47	    itself built from an expanding-window AR(5) (`flow_transform/unexpected_z`)
    48	    therefore does NOT satisfy it, is flagged as such in the registry
    49	    (`bounded_memory=false`), and is reported with a sensitivity that re-runs the
    50	    full-family correction without it. None of the 10 primary cells uses it.
    51	  * A pre-specified one-sided MATERIAL-GAIN EXCLUSION test. Failing to reject
    52	    equal accuracy is not evidence of equality; only this test can license a
    53	    bounded null, and only in QLIKE-loss space.
    54	  * Clark-West is retained but reported strictly as evidence about a SEPARATE
    55	    MSPE estimand. It is never relabelled as a QLIKE general-loss test.
    56	
    57	Power is reported (>=1000 simulated OOS paths, with a false-positive check at
    58	beta = 0) but it is a statement about the DESIGN, not about the effect: power
    59	cannot prove that the effect is smaller than the minimum detectable effect. v1
    60	made exactly that inversion. Its SCOPE is deliberately narrow and must be quoted
    61	as such: h = 1 only (the primary family also contains h = 5), a single injected
    62	|flow| shock (the H2 asymmetry and the cross-asset H4 alternatives are NOT
    63	simulated), and a SINGLE-CELL nominal gate. It is NOT the power of the ten-cell
    64	Holm-corrected family, which is strictly lower. The beta = 0 row is a
    65	FALSE-POSITIVE DIAGNOSTIC, not a size calibration -- see `false_positive_note`.
    66	
    67	Information set / lookahead
    68	---------------------------
    69	Farside publishes day-t flows after the US close (~20:00-21:00 UTC on day t).
    70	We therefore set the forecast origin at 00:00 UTC ending calendar day t:
    71	    - flow_t          known (published ~21:00 UTC, i.e. before 24:00 UTC)
    72	    - RV_t (UTC day)  known (the UTC day just closed)
    73	    - target RV_{t+1} lies entirely in the future
    74	State controls and the flow carry SEPARATE lags (`state_lag`, `flow_lag`): if a
    75	flow only becomes usable one day later, that does not un-observe yesterday's
    76	realized volatility.

   137	# GW's asymptotics require BOUNDED estimator memory: the forecasting *method*
   138	# must re-estimate on a fixed-length rolling window, not an expanding one. 250
   139	# flow days (~1 trading year) is the longest window that still leaves ETH -- the
   140	# shorter series -- with enough origins for the test's n>=60 requirement.

   150	# equation, so a one-sd flow shock multiplies RV by exp(beta).

   156	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   157	# 0. Test registry — the single source of the multiple-testing family (C6)
   158	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   159	@dataclass
   160	class TestRecord:
   161	    """One statistical test. The family is DERIVED from this list, never hand-listed.
   162	
   163	    v1 hand-enumerated "EVERY DM test" and silently missed the 8 smearing tests.
   164	    Every test in this study now registers itself here, and the multiple-testing
   165	    families are built by filtering this registry.
   166	
   167	    `feeds_gate` is the honest bit: only tests whose inference is valid for a
   168	    nested comparison are allowed to reach the verdict. Raw Diebold-Mariano
   169	    records are registered with feeds_gate=False so they show up in the audit
   170	    trail without contaminating the claim.
   171	
   172	    `p_one_sided` is stored RAW (unrounded). v1 fed 4-decimal rounded p-values
   173	    into Holm.
   174	
   175	    `bounded_memory` records whether the FORECASTING METHOD behind this test has
   176	    the bounded estimator memory Giacomini-White assumes. It is a property of the
   177	    whole method, not of the final regression: a cell whose regressor is itself
   178	    built by an expanding-window fit does not have it, even though every column
   179	    is strictly backward-looking. Flagging it here is what lets the write-up stop
   180	    claiming, in one blanket sentence, that every registered test is a
   181	    bounded-memory test -- and lets the family-wise correction be re-run without
   182	    the offender as a published sensitivity rather than a quiet exclusion.
   183	    """
   184	
   185	    family: str
   186	    cell: str
   187	    asset: str
   188	    horizon: int
   189	    base: str
   190	    alt: str
   191	    inference: str
   192	    estimand: str
   193	    scheme: str
   194	    stat: float
   195	    p_one_sided: float
   196	    feeds_gate: bool
   197	    qlike_improve_pct: float | None = None
   198	    n: int | None = None
   199	    bounded_memory: bool = True
   200	
   201	
   202	REGISTRY: list[TestRecord] = []
   203	
   204	# Paired QLIKE loss streams, keyed by cell. Kept so a confidence bound can be
   205	# re-inverted at any alpha AFTER the family size is known (the Bonferroni level
   206	# depends on how many cells there turned out to be). Never serialized.
   207	LOSS_CACHE: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int]] = {}
   208	
   209	
   210	def register(rec: TestRecord) -> TestRecord:
   211	    REGISTRY.append(rec)
   212	    return rec
   213	
   214	
   215	def qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(cell: str, alpha: float) -> float | None:
   216	    la, lb, h = LOSS_CACHE[cell]
   217	    return qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=alpha)
   218	
   219	
   220	def _assert_unique_cell(
   221	    family: str,
   222	    p,
   223	    alt: str,
   224	    smearing: str,
   225	    train_window: int | None,
   226	    variant: str,
   227	) -> str:
   228	    """Build the cell key and refuse to let two cells share one.
   229	
   230	    The key joins the registry, the loss cache and the verdict. When it was just
   231	    `{asset}_h{h}_{alt}`, the same string named the primary BTC h=1 cell AND its
   232	    rv-proxy, flow-lag, threshold and window variants -- so the dict that carried
   233	    exclusion statistics into the verdict silently kept whichever cell was written
   234	    LAST. The primary family was being adjudicated with a robustness cell's
   235	    numbers. Uniqueness is now a precondition, not a hope.
   236	    """
   237	    parts = [family, f"{p.asset}_h{p.horizon}", alt, p.rv_col, f"fl{p.flow_lag}"]
   238	    if smearing != "own":
   239	        parts.append(smearing)
   240	    if train_window != GW_TRAIN_WINDOW:
   241	        parts.append(f"tw{train_window}")
   242	    if variant:
   243	        parts.append(variant)
   244	    cell = "|".join(parts)
   245	    if cell in LOSS_CACHE:
   246	        raise AssertionError(
   247	            f"duplicate cell key {cell!r}: two comparisons would collide in the "
   248	            "registry/loss-cache join. Pass a distinguishing `variant=`."
   249	        )
   250	    return cell
   251	
   252	
   253	def holm(pvals: list[float]) -> list[float]:
   254	    """Holm step-down. Takes RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."""
   255	    m = len(pvals)
   256	    if m == 0:
   257	        return []
   258	    order = np.argsort(pvals)
   259	    adj = np.empty(m)
   260	    running = 0.0
   261	    for rank, idx in enumerate(order):
   262	        running = max(running, (m - rank) * pvals[idx])
   263	        adj[idx] = min(running, 1.0)
   264	    return [float(a) for a in adj]
   265	
   266	
   267	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   268	# 1. US equity session calendar (C3)
   269	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   270	def us_equity_sessions(start: pd.Timestamp, end: pd.Timestamp) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
   271	    """NYSE session days over [start, end].
   272	
   273	    Farside keeps a row for US market holidays with every fund column showing a
   274	    dash and `Total` showing 0.0. `sum(skipna=True)` over an all-NaN row is 0.0,
   275	    so the parser cross-check cannot see it either: the holiday looks exactly
   276	    like a genuine zero-flow day. Those fake zeros then enter the 20-flow-day
   277	    rolling sd that scales every shock.
   278	
   279	    A "flow day" is by construction a day the ETFs could trade, i.e. an NYSE
   280	    session. Anything else is MISSING, not zero.

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba experiments/k1709/k1709.py | sed -n '280,820p'" in /Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709
 succeeded in 0ms:
   280	    session. Anything else is MISSING, not zero.
   281	    """
   282	    cal = xcals.get_calendar("XNYS")
   283	    sess = cal.sessions_in_range(
   284	        pd.Timestamp(start).normalize(), pd.Timestamp(end).normalize()
   285	    )
   286	    idx = pd.DatetimeIndex(sess)
   287	    if idx.tz is not None:
   288	        idx = idx.tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None)
   289	    return idx.normalize()
   290	
   291	
   292	def filter_to_sessions(df: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   293	    """Keep only NYSE session rows; report exactly what was dropped.
   294	
   295	    Split out of `fetch_flows` so it can be tested without hitting the network:
   296	    a data-integrity rule that only runs behind an HTTP call is a rule nobody
   297	    ever tests.
   298	    """
   299	    sessions = us_equity_sessions(df.index.min(), df.index.max())
   300	    is_session = df.index.isin(sessions)
   301	    dropped = df.index[~is_session]
   302	    detail = [
   303	        {
   304	            "date": str(d.date()),
   305	            "weekday": d.day_name(),
   306	            "farside_total_musd": float(df.loc[d, "flow"]),
   307	            "all_fund_columns_dash": (
   308	                bool(df.loc[d, "all_dash"]) if "all_dash" in df.columns else None
   309	            ),
   310	        }
   311	        for d in dropped
   312	    ]
   313	    diag = {
   314	        "n_nonsession_rows_dropped": len(detail),
   315	        "n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total": int(
   316	            (df.loc[~is_session, "flow"].abs() > 1e-9).sum()
   317	        ),
   318	        "nonsession_rows_dropped": detail,
   319	    }
   320	    keep = df[is_session]
   321	    if "all_dash" in keep.columns:
   322	        keep = keep.drop(columns=["all_dash"])
   323	    return keep, diag
   324

   326	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   327	# 2. Farside flow parsing (the traps live here)
   328	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   329	def _parse_money(x) -> float:
   330	    """Farside cell -> float ($M).
   331	
   332	    Traps handled:
   333	      '(95.1)'  -> -95.1      negative flows are written in parentheses
   334	      '(27,332)'-> -27332.0   thousands separators
   335	      '-' / '–' -> NaN        fund not trading / no data  (NOT zero)
   336	      '0.0'     -> 0.0        a genuine zero flow  (NOT missing)
   337	    """
   338	    if x is None or (isinstance(x, float) and np.isnan(x)):
   339	        return np.nan
   340	    s = str(x).strip()
   341	    # em/en dash and bare hyphen mean "no data", not zero.
   342	    if s in {"", "-", "–", "—", "nan", "NaN"}:
   343	        return np.nan
   344	    neg = s.startswith("(") and s.endswith(")")
   345	    s = s.strip("()").replace(",", "").replace("$", "").replace("*", "")
   346	    try:
   347	        v = float(s)
   348	    except ValueError:
   349	        return np.nan
   350	    return -v if neg else v
   351	
   352	
   353	def fetch_flows(asset: str) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   354	    """Download and parse the Farside all-data flow table for `asset`."""
   355	    resp = requests.get(FARSIDE[asset], headers=UA, timeout=60)
   356	    resp.raise_for_status()
   357	    tables = pd.read_html(io.StringIO(resp.text))
   358	    raw = max(tables, key=lambda t: t.shape[0] * t.shape[1])
   359	    n_raw = len(raw)
   360	
   361	    # ETH ships a MultiIndex header (issuer / ticker / fee). Keep the ticker row.
   362	    if isinstance(raw.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   363	        raw.columns = [
   364	            c[1] if not str(c[1]).startswith("Unnamed") else c[0] for c in raw.columns
   365	        ]
   366	    raw.columns = [str(c).strip() for c in raw.columns]
   367	    date_col = raw.columns[0]
   368	    raw = raw.rename(columns={date_col: "Date"})
   369	
   370	    # Drop the 'Total' footer row and the 'Seed' row (seed capital, not daily flow).
   371	    lbl = raw["Date"].astype(str).str.strip()
   372	    n_total_rows = int(lbl.str.fullmatch("Total", case=False).sum())
   373	    n_seed_rows = int(lbl.str.fullmatch("Seed", case=False).sum())
   374	    raw = raw[~lbl.str.fullmatch("Total|Seed", case=False)]
   375	
   376	    dt = pd.to_datetime(raw["Date"], format="%d %b %Y", errors="coerce")
   377	    n_unparsed = int(dt.isna().sum())
   378	    raw = raw.assign(date=dt).dropna(subset=["date"])
   379	
   380	    fund_cols = [c for c in raw.columns if c not in {"Date", "date", "Total"}]
   381	    parsed = raw[fund_cols].map(_parse_money)
   382	    total = raw["Total"].map(_parse_money)
   383	
   384	    # Parser self-check: Farside's own Total must equal the sum of the fund
   385	    # columns (NaN = fund not trading = contributes nothing). A mismatch means
   386	    # the paren/comma/dash handling above is wrong -- fail loud, do not proceed.
   387	    #
   388	    # NOTE this check is BLIND to the market-holiday trap: an all-dash row sums
   389	    # to 0.0 and Farside's own Total also reads 0.0, so the residual is 0. That
   390	    # is precisely why the session-calendar filter below is a separate layer.
   391	    recomputed = parsed.sum(axis=1, skipna=True)
   392	    resid = (total - recomputed).abs()
   393	    max_resid = float(resid.max())
   394	    if max_resid > 1.0:  # $1M tolerance for Farside's own rounding
   395	        worst = resid.idxmax()
   396	        raise AssertionError(
   397	            f"[{asset}] flow parser failed cross-check: max |Total - sum(funds)| = "
   398	            f"{max_resid:.3f} $M on {raw.loc[worst, 'date'].date()}"
   399	        )
   400	
   401	    # Gross churn = sum of |fund-level flow|. Net flow can be ~0 while one issuer
   402	    # creates and another redeems heavily -- that is a real liquidity event that
   403	    # the NET series is blind to. Keeping it lets us test whether the null is an
   404	    # artifact of netting (Warther 1995: only flow INNOVATIONS should inform).
   405	    gross = parsed.abs().sum(axis=1, skipna=True)
   406	    all_dash = parsed.isna().all(axis=1)
   407	
   408	    df = pd.DataFrame(
   409	        {"flow": total.values, "gross": gross.values, "all_dash": all_dash.values},
   410	        index=pd.DatetimeIndex(raw["date"].values),
   411	    )
   412	    df = df.sort_index()
   413	    df = df[~df.index.duplicated(keep="last")]
   414	    df = df.dropna(subset=["flow"])
   415	
   416	    df, session_diag = filter_to_sessions(df)
   417	    dropped_detail = session_diag["nonsession_rows_dropped"]
   418	    n_dropped_nonzero = session_diag["n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total"]
   419	
   420	    diag = {
   421	        "source_url": FARSIDE[asset],
   422	        "raw_rows": n_raw,
   423	        "dropped_total_rows": n_total_rows,
   424	        "dropped_seed_rows": n_seed_rows,
   425	        "dropped_unparseable_dates": n_unparsed,
   426	        "session_calendar": "XNYS (exchange_calendars)",
   427	        "n_nonsession_rows_dropped": len(dropped_detail),
   428	        "n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total": n_dropped_nonzero,
   429	        "nonsession_rows_dropped": dropped_detail,
   430	        "session_filter_note": (
   431	            "Farside emits a row for every US market holiday with all fund "
   432	            "columns dashed and Total = 0.0. skipna summation makes the parser "
   433	            "cross-check read 0.0 - 0.0 = 0, so it cannot detect them. These are "
   434	            "MISSING flow days, not genuine zero-flow days, and they were "
   435	            "polluting the 20-flow-day rolling sd used to scale every shock."
   436	        ),
   437	        "n_obs": int(len(df)),
   438	        "date_min": str(df.index.min().date()),
   439	        "date_max": str(df.index.max().date()),
   440	        "parser_crosscheck_max_abs_resid_musd": round(max_resid, 4),
   441	        "total_flow_musd": {
   442	            "mean": round(float(df["flow"].mean()), 3),
   443	            "std": round(float(df["flow"].std()), 3),
   444	            "min": round(float(df["flow"].min()), 3),
   445	            "max": round(float(df["flow"].max()), 3),
   446	            "median": round(float(df["flow"].median()), 3),
   447	        },
   448	        "share_negative": round(float((df["flow"] < 0).mean()), 4),
   449	        "share_exact_zero_on_session_days": round(float((df["flow"] == 0).mean()), 4),
   450	        "n_fund_columns": len(fund_cols),
   451	    }
   452	    return df, diag
   453	
   454	
   455	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   456	# 3. Realized volatility proxies
   457	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   458	def fetch_rv(asset: str) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   459	    """Calendar-daily (UTC) RV proxies for `asset`.
   460	
   461	    Primary  : Garman-Klass variance from OHLC (spans the full sample).
   462	    Robust 1 : true realized variance from 24 hourly log returns (2024-07-15+).
   463	    Robust 2 : squared daily close-to-close log return.
   464	
   465	    Note on the hourly RV: for a 24/7 market there is no session gap, so the
   466	    return spanning the UTC midnight boundary is a genuine return, not an
   467	    overnight jump. We therefore diff the *continuous* hourly series and assign
   468	    each return to the UTC date of its end stamp (24 returns/day). This is the
   469	    opposite of the 2026-07-11 0050.TW fix, and deliberately so: that market
   470	    closes overnight, this one does not. Days without exactly 24 returns are
   471	    dropped to avoid a scaling bias.
   472	    """
   473	    px = yf.download(
   474	        TICKER[asset], start="2023-06-01", interval="1d", progress=False, auto_adjust=False
   475	    )
   476	    if isinstance(px.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   477	        px.columns = px.columns.get_level_values(0)
   478	    px = px[["Open", "High", "Low", "Close"]].dropna()
   479	    px.index = pd.DatetimeIndex(px.index).tz_localize(None).normalize()
   480	
   481	    # (a) Drop the still-open current UTC day. Yahoo serves a PARTIAL bar for
   482	    #     today, whose High/Low have not finished widening -- it systematically
   483	    #     understates GK variance. Only fully-closed UTC days may enter.
   484	    today_utc = pd.Timestamp.utcnow().tz_localize(None).normalize()
   485	    n_open_bar = int((px.index >= today_utc).sum())
   486	    px = px[px.index < today_utc]
   487	
   488	    # (b) Reindex onto a COMPLETE daily calendar. BTC/ETH trade 24/7, so every
   489	    #     calendar day should exist -- but Yahoo does drop days (2026-07-13 was
   490	    #     missing at the time of writing). Without this, `.shift(1)` would shift
   491	    #     by ROW POSITION, not by one calendar day, and a gap would silently turn
   492	    #     a t+1 target into a t+2 target. Missing days become NaN and are dropped
   493	    #     from the panel, instead of compressing the timeline.
   494	    full = pd.date_range(px.index.min(), px.index.max(), freq="D")
   495	    n_missing = int(len(full.difference(px.index)))
   496	    missing_days = [str(d.date()) for d in full.difference(px.index)]
   497	    px = px.reindex(full)
   498	
   499	    ln_hl = np.log(px["High"] / px["Low"])
   500	    ln_co = np.log(px["Close"] / px["Open"])
   501	    gk = 0.5 * ln_hl**2 - (2 * np.log(2) - 1) * ln_co**2
   502	    park = ln_hl**2 / (4 * np.log(2))
   503	    ret = np.log(px["Close"]).diff()
   504	
   505	    out = pd.DataFrame(
   506	        {"rv_gk": gk, "rv_park": park, "rv_r2": ret**2, "ret": ret}, index=px.index
   507	    )
   508	
   509	    # --- hourly true RV -----------------------------------------------------
   510	    hr = yf.download(
   511	        TICKER[asset], interval="1h", period="730d", progress=False, auto_adjust=False
   512	    )
   513	    n_hourly_days = 0
   514	    if len(hr):
   515	        if isinstance(hr.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   516	            hr.columns = hr.columns.get_level_values(0)
   517	        close = hr["Close"].dropna().sort_index()
   518	        r = np.log(close).diff().dropna()               # continuous: 24/7, no gap
   519	        day = pd.DatetimeIndex(r.index).tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None).normalize()
   520	        grp = pd.DataFrame({"r2": r.values**2, "day": day}).groupby("day")
   521	        rv_h = grp["r2"].sum()
   522	        cnt = grp["r2"].size()
   523	        rv_h = rv_h[cnt == 24]                          # complete UTC days only
   524	        n_hourly_days = int(len(rv_h))
   525	        out["rv_hourly"] = rv_h.reindex(out.index)
   526	    else:
   527	        out["rv_hourly"] = np.nan
   528	
   529	    neg_gk = int((out["rv_gk"] < 0).sum())
   530	    # QLIKE has a -log(actual) term, so a near-zero "actual" blows it up. Track
   531	    # how many observations each proxy floors: r^2 is a chi^2(1) proxy and hits
   532	    # near-zero on quiet days, which is one reason it is a NOISY variance target
   533	    # and is reported only as robustness, never as the headline.
   534	    clipped = {c: int((out[c] < EPS).sum()) for c in ("rv_gk", "rv_park", "rv_r2", "rv_hourly")}
   535	    for c in ("rv_gk", "rv_park", "rv_r2", "rv_hourly"):
   536	        out[c] = out[c].clip(lower=EPS)
   537	
   538	    diag = {
   539	        "ticker": TICKER[asset],
   540	        "n_daily_obs": int(len(out)),
   541	        "date_min": str(out.index.min().date()),
   542	        "date_max": str(out.index.max().date()),
   543	        "n_negative_gk_clipped": neg_gk,
   544	        "n_open_partial_bars_dropped": n_open_bar,
   545	        "n_missing_calendar_days_reindexed": n_missing,
   546	        "missing_calendar_days": missing_days,
   547	        "n_obs_floored_at_eps_by_proxy": clipped,
   548	        "qlike_noise_note": (
   549	            "r^2 is a chi^2(1) variance proxy: its QLIKE level runs ~7x that of "
   550	            "hourly RV, so it is reported as robustness only."
   551	        ),
   552	        "n_hourly_complete_days": n_hourly_days,
   553	        "hourly_coverage_start": (
   554	            str(out["rv_hourly"].dropna().index.min().date())
   555	            if out["rv_hourly"].notna().any()
   556	            else None
   557	        ),
   558	        "ann_vol_gk_pct": round(float(np.sqrt(out["rv_gk"].mean() * 365) * 100), 2),
   559	        "corr_gk_vs_hourly": (
   560	            round(
   561	                float(np.log(out[["rv_gk", "rv_hourly"]].dropna()).corr().iloc[0, 1]), 4
   562	            )
   563	            if out["rv_hourly"].notna().sum() > 30
   564	            else None
   565	        ),
   566	    }
   567	    return out, diag
   568	
   569	
   570	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   571	# 4. Flow features — computed ONCE, reused by every panel
   572	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   573	@dataclass
   574	class FlowFeatures:
   575	    """Flow-derived regressors, indexed by FLOW DAY (= NYSE session).
   576	
   577	    These depend only on the flow series, never on RV, so they are computed once
   578	    and reused. That matters for the power simulation, which rebuilds the RV side
   579	    thousands of times: recomputing the rolling AR(5) each rep would dominate the
   580	    runtime and, worse, tempt a divergent panel-construction path.
   581	    """
   582	
   583	    flow: pd.Series
   584	    z: pd.Series
   585	    z_unexp: pd.Series
   586	    z_gross: pd.Series | None = None
   587	
   588	
   589	def flow_zscore(flow: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
   590	    """Scale each flow by the sd of the previous Z_WINDOW *flow days*.
   591	
   592	    `.shift(1)` on the rolling sd keeps the scaler strictly backward-looking:
   593	    z_t = flow_t / sd(flow_{t-20..t-1}), so nothing on day t enters its own
   594	    denominator. The window is indexed by flow days (weekends and US market
   595	    holidays carry no flow), which is why this is computed BEFORE reindexing
   596	    onto the 24/7 crypto calendar.
   597	    """
   598	    sd_prior = flow.rolling(Z_WINDOW).std().shift(1)
   599	    return flow / sd_prior
   600	
   601	
   602	def ar_residual(flow: pd.Series, p: int = 5) -> pd.Series:
   603	    """Rolling AR(p) forecast error of `flow` -- the 'surprise' in each day's flow.
   604	
   605	    The AR is refitted every day on a STRICTLY PRIOR window (targets f[p:i], so
   606	    day i's own value never enters its own fit). The design matrix columns are
   607	    ordered [lag1, lag2, ..., lagp], so the prediction vector must be
   608	    f[i-1], f[i-2], ..., f[i-p] -- the reversed slice below produces exactly
   609	    that. Getting this ordering wrong silently multiplies the lag-p coefficient
   610	    by the lag-1 value; `test_unexpected_flow_ar5_lag_ordering` guards it.
   611	    """
   612	    f = flow.to_numpy(float)
   613	    n = len(f)
   614	    resid = np.full(n, np.nan)
   615	    for i in range(Z_WINDOW + p, n):
   616	        y = f[p:i]                                   # targets strictly before i
   617	        X = np.column_stack([f[p - k : i - k] for k in range(1, p + 1)])
   618	        if len(y) < 40:
   619	            continue
   620	        Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(y)), X])
   621	        beta = np.linalg.lstsq(Xc, y, rcond=None)[0]
   622	        lags = f[i - 1 : i - p - 1 : -1] if i - p - 1 >= 0 else f[i - 1 :: -1][:p]
   623	        resid[i] = f[i] - float(np.r_[1.0, lags] @ beta)   # surprise on day i
   624	    return pd.Series(resid, index=flow.index)
   625	
   626	
   627	def make_flow_features(flow: pd.DataFrame) -> FlowFeatures:
   628	    """Warther (1995): only the UNEXPECTED component of flow should carry news.
   629	
   630	    Flow is strongly autocorrelated, so raw net flow is largely predictable from
   631	    its own past. We strip that out with the rolling AR(5) and z-score the
   632	    residual by its own strictly-prior rolling sd. If our null were merely an
   633	    artifact of feeding the model the *predictable* part of flow, this variable
   634	    would rescue it.
   635	    """
   636	    # CAVEAT (Codex, rev1 review). This AR(5) refits on an EXPANDING window of flow
   637	    # history. The regressor is still strictly backward-looking -- day i's own value
   638	    # never enters its own fit, so there is no lookahead -- but the FORECASTING
   639	    # METHOD that uses `z_unexp` therefore does not have bounded estimator memory.
   640	    # GW's limiting experiment formally wants it to. The 10 primary cells do not use
   641	    # this column at all; only the `flow_transform/unexpected_z` robustness cell
   642	    # does, and its GW p-value is reported with that caveat attached rather than
   643	    # being quietly counted as a bounded-memory test.
   644	    r = ar_residual(flow["flow"], p=5)
   645	    return FlowFeatures(
   646	        flow=flow["flow"],
   647	        z=flow_zscore(flow["flow"]),
   648	        z_unexp=r / r.rolling(Z_WINDOW).std().shift(1),
   649	        z_gross=flow_zscore(flow["gross"]) if "gross" in flow.columns else None,
   650	    )
   651	
   652	
   653	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   654	# 5. Panel construction — the alignment layer
   655	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   656	@dataclass
   657	class Panel:
   658	    df: pd.DataFrame           # indexed by TARGET date tau
   659	    rv_col: str
   660	    horizon: int
   661	    asset: str
   662	    state_lag: int = STATE_LAG
   663	    flow_lag: int = 1
   664	
   665	
   666	STATE_COLS = ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"]
   667	FLOW_COLS = ["flow", "z", "z_unexp", "z_gross", "z_btc"]
   668	
   669	
   670	def assert_calendar_is_complete(idx: pd.DatetimeIndex, label: str) -> None:
   671	    """Guard the assumption every `.shift()` in this file relies on.
   672	
   673	    `.shift(k)` moves by ROW POSITION. It equals "k calendar days back" only if
   674	    the index is a complete, sorted, duplicate-free daily range. Codex's review
   675	    showed `assert_no_lookahead` alone could not prove this: it re-checks the
   676	    same src_date the shift itself produced. This checks the PRECONDITION, before
   677	    any rolling or shifting happens.
   678	    """
   679	    if not idx.is_monotonic_increasing:
   680	        raise AssertionError(f"[{label}] calendar index is not sorted")
   681	    if idx.has_duplicates:
   682	        dupes = idx[idx.duplicated()][:5]
   683	        raise AssertionError(f"[{label}] duplicate calendar dates: {list(dupes)}")
   684	    full = pd.date_range(idx.min(), idx.max(), freq="D")
   685	    if len(full) != len(idx) or not full.equals(pd.DatetimeIndex(idx)):
   686	        missing = full.difference(idx)[:5]
   687	        raise AssertionError(
   688	            f"[{label}] calendar has {len(full) - len(idx)} hole(s); "
   689	            f"`.shift()` would move by row position, not by one day. "
   690	            f"First missing: {list(missing)}"
   691	        )
   692	
   693	
   694	def build_panel(
   695	    rv: pd.DataFrame,
   696	    ff: FlowFeatures,
   697	    rv_col: str,
   698	    horizon: int,
   699	    asset: str,
   700	    flow_lag: int = 1,
   701	    state_lag: int = STATE_LAG,
   702	    btc_z: pd.Series | None = None,
   703	) -> Panel:
   704	    """Assemble the design matrix, indexed by the *target* date tau.
   705	
   706	    C4 fix. v1 had a single `pub_lag` that shifted EVERYTHING, so the
   707	    conservative "flow is only published at the end of day t+1" run also pushed
   708	    the HAR and return controls back to t-1 -- but RV_{t+1} and ret_{t+1} are
   709	    obviously known by then. That handicapped the baseline and made the
   710	    robustness run answer a question nobody asked.
   711	
   712	    Now:
   713	      state_lag : RV/return controls. ALWAYS 1 -- yesterday's realized
   714	                  volatility is known today, whatever the flow vendor does.
   715	      flow_lag  : 1 (baseline: flow_t usable from the end of day t)
   716	                  2 (conservative: flow_t only usable from the end of day t+1)
   717	    """
   718	    assert_calendar_is_complete(pd.DatetimeIndex(rv.index), f"{asset} rv")
   719	    cal = rv.copy()
   720	
   721	    # --- HAR features, computed on calendar days, as of the END of each day ---
   722	    lr = np.log(cal[rv_col])
   723	    cal["har_d"] = lr
   724	    cal["har_w"] = lr.rolling(HAR_W).mean()
   725	    cal["har_m"] = lr.rolling(HAR_M).mean()
   726	    cal["abs_ret"] = cal["ret"].abs()
   727	
   728	    # --- flow shocks, reindexed from flow-day space onto the 24/7 calendar ---
   729	    cal["flow"] = ff.flow.reindex(cal.index)     # NaN on non-flow calendar days
   730	    cal["z"] = ff.z.reindex(cal.index)
   731	    cal["z_unexp"] = ff.z_unexp.reindex(cal.index)
   732	    if ff.z_gross is not None:
   733	        cal["z_gross"] = ff.z_gross.reindex(cal.index)
   734	    if btc_z is not None:
   735	        cal["z_btc"] = btc_z.reindex(cal.index)
   736	
   737	    # --- the two visible lags ------------------------------------------------
   738	    state = [c for c in STATE_COLS if c in cal.columns]
   739	    flow_c = [c for c in FLOW_COLS if c in cal.columns]
   740	    day = pd.Series(cal.index, index=cal.index)
   741	
   742	    X = cal[state].shift(state_lag)
   743	    X[flow_c] = cal[flow_c].shift(flow_lag)
   744	    X["state_src_date"] = day.shift(state_lag)
   745	    X["flow_src_date"] = day.shift(flow_lag)
   746	
   747	    # --- target: average RV over the next `horizon` calendar days ------------
   748	    # tau is the FIRST day of the target window; the window is [tau, tau+h-1].
   749	    fwd = cal[rv_col].rolling(horizon).mean().shift(-(horizon - 1))
   750	    X["y"] = fwd
   751	    X["y_end_date"] = day.shift(-(horizon - 1))
   752	
   753	    # Derived flow-shock regressors
   754	    X["abs_z"] = X["z"].abs()
   755	    X["z_neg"] = X["abs_z"] * (X["z"] < 0)     # extra loading on redemptions
   756	    X["z_signed"] = X["z"]                     # directional (not magnitude)
   757	    X["z_sq"] = X["z"] ** 2                    # convex in shock size
   758	    X["abs_z_unexp"] = X["z_unexp"].abs()      # Warther: unexpected component
   759	    if "z_gross" in X.columns:
   760	        X["abs_z_gross"] = X["z_gross"].abs()  # gross churn, blind to netting
   761	    if btc_z is not None:
   762	        X["abs_z_btc"] = X["z_btc"].abs()
   763	
   764	    X["dow_src"] = X["flow_src_date"].dt.dayofweek   # 4 = Friday flow day
   765	    panel = X.dropna(subset=["y", "har_m", "z", "y_end_date"])
   766	    return Panel(panel, rv_col, horizon, asset, state_lag=state_lag, flow_lag=flow_lag)
   767	
   768	
   769	def assert_no_lookahead(p: Panel) -> None:
   770	    """Re-derive the timing guarantee from the data itself.
   771	
   772	    Checks, per Codex's review:
   773	      (1) state gap == state_lag exactly. `<` would be lookahead; `>` would mean
   774	          `.shift()` moved by row position across a calendar hole, silently
   775	          turning a t+1 target into a t+2 target. An inequality check would miss
   776	          the second, which is exactly how the 2026-07-13 Yahoo gap slipped past.
   777	      (2) flow gap == flow_lag exactly, verified SEPARATELY. v1 only had one
   778	          src_date, so it could not have caught a state/flow lag mixup.
   779	      (3) the target window closes exactly h-1 days after it opens -- not merely
   780	          "at or after". A calendar hole inside the rolling target window would
   781	          stretch a 5-day window across 6 calendar days and the old `>=` check
   782	          would have waved it through.
   783	    """
   784	    d = p.df
   785	    for src, lag, name in (
   786	        ("state_src_date", p.state_lag, "state"),
   787	        ("flow_src_date", p.flow_lag, "flow"),
   788	    ):
   789	        gap = (d.index - d[src]).dt.days
   790	        if (gap != lag).any():
   791	            bad = d.loc[gap != lag]
   792	            raise AssertionError(
   793	                f"[{p.asset} h={p.horizon}] {len(bad)} rows have a {name} "
   794	                f"source->target gap != {lag} day(s) "
   795	                f"(min={gap.min()}, max={gap.max()}). First offender: "
   796	                f"target={bad.index[0].date()} src={bad[src].iloc[0].date()}"
   797	            )
   798	    span = (d["y_end_date"] - d.index).dt.days
   799	    if (span != p.horizon - 1).any():
   800	        bad = d.loc[span != p.horizon - 1]
   801	        raise AssertionError(
   802	            f"[{p.asset} h={p.horizon}] {len(bad)} target windows do not span "
   803	            f"exactly {p.horizon} calendar days (min={span.min()}, max={span.max()})"
   804	        )
   805	
   806	
   807	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   808	# 6. Estimation
   809	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   810	SPECS: dict[str, list[str]] = {
   811	    "HAR":        ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m"],
   812	    "HAR+ctrl":   ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"],
   813	    "H1_absflow": ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z"],
   814	    "H2_asym":    ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z", "z_neg"],
   815	    "H4_own":     ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z"],
   816	    "H4_plus_btc": [
   817	        "har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z", "abs_z_btc",
   818	    ],
   819	}

   821	
   822	def _ols(X: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
   823	    return np.linalg.lstsq(np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]), y, rcond=None)[0]
   824	
   825	
   826	def _hac_se(X: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray, beta: np.ndarray, lag: int) -> np.ndarray:
   827	    """Newey-West standard errors (needed: overlapping targets when h>1)."""
   828	    Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X])
   829	    u = y - Xc @ beta
   830	    XtX_inv = np.linalg.pinv(Xc.T @ Xc)
   831	    S = (Xc * u[:, None]).T @ (Xc * u[:, None])
   832	    for L in range(1, lag + 1):
   833	        w = 1 - L / (lag + 1)
   834	        A = (Xc[L:] * u[L:, None]).T @ (Xc[:-L] * u[:-L, None])
   835	        S += w * (A + A.T)
   836	    V = XtX_inv @ S @ XtX_inv
   837	    return np.sqrt(np.maximum(np.diag(V), 0))
   838	
   839	
   840	def canonical_bandwidth(h: int, n: int) -> int:
   841	    """The repo's canonical HAC bandwidth (see .claude/rules/experiments.md).
   842	
   843	    `h-1` alone degenerates to ZERO at h=1, i.e. no HAC at all, which is the
   844	    K1655 trap. Every long-run variance in this file uses max(h-1, this).
   845	    """
   846	    return max(1, min(int(np.ceil(h ** (1 / 3) * n ** (1 / 3))), n // 4))
   847	
   848	
   849	def in_sample(p: Panel, spec: str) -> dict:
   850	    cols = SPECS[spec]
   851	    d = p.df.dropna(subset=cols)
   852	    X = d[cols].to_numpy(float)
   853	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   854	    beta = _ols(X, y)
   855	    lag = max(p.horizon - 1, canonical_bandwidth(p.horizon, len(d)))
   856	    se = _hac_se(X, y, beta, lag)
   857	    names = ["const"] + cols
   858	    yhat = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]) @ beta
   859	    ss = 1 - np.sum((y - yhat) ** 2) / np.sum((y - y.mean()) ** 2)
   860	    return {
   861	        "spec": spec,
   862	        "n": int(len(d)),
   863	        "r2": round(float(ss), 4),
   864	        "hac_lag": int(lag),
   865	        "coef": {
   866	            nm: {"beta": round(float(b), 5), "t": round(float(b / s) if s > 0 else 0.0, 3)}
   867	            for nm, b, s in zip(names, beta, se)
   868	        },
   869	    }
   870	
   871	
   872	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   873	# 7. Paired fixed-window OOS — the estimator GW requires (C1)
   874	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   875	@dataclass
   876	class PairedOOS:
   877	    actual: np.ndarray
   878	    pred_base: np.ndarray
   879	    pred_aug: np.ndarray
   880	    origins: pd.DatetimeIndex
   881	    audit: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
   882	
   883	
   884	def paired_oos(
   885	    p: Panel,
   886	    base: str,
   887	    alt: str,
   888	    train_window: int | None = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
   889	    smearing: str = "own",
   890	) -> PairedOOS:
   891	    """Both models, one estimation window, identical training dates.
   892	
   893	    `train_window=None` reproduces v1's EXPANDING window. It is retained for one
   894	    purpose only: to show, in the results file, what the design change did to the
   895	    statistic. Expanding-window losses never feed a verdict here -- see
   896	    `giacomini_white` for why the scheme is the whole ballgame.
   897	
   898	    Why fixed and not expanding (this is the whole point of the C1 fix):
   899	    Giacomini-White (2006) tests equal predictive ability of FORECASTING METHODS
   900	    -- estimation error included -- and its asymptotics need the estimator to
   901	    have bounded memory. An expanding window does not. v1 ran an expanding
   902	    window and then applied a test whose null assumed otherwise.
   903	
   904	    Both specs are fit on the AUGMENTED model's complete-case mask, so they see
   905	    the exact same rows, the exact same targets, and the exact same embargo. That
   906	    common sample is part of the nested comparison, not a cosmetic detail.
   907	
   908	    Embargo: with overlapping h-day targets, "every row before i" would let the
   909	    model train on a label window that overlaps the forecast day. A training row
   910	    is eligible at origin i only once its whole label window has closed:
   911	    `y_end_date < origin_i`.
   912	
   913	    Smearing (log -> variance level). The augmented spec has more parameters ->
   914	    lower training residual variance -> a smaller exp(s^2/2) multiplier -> lower
   915	    variance forecasts. QLIKE is asymmetric, so this channel could in principle
   916	    MANUFACTURE the null we are reporting.
   917	      "own"    -- each spec uses its own s^2  (primary)
   918	      "none"   -- no smearing at all, forecast = exp(mu)
   919	      "shared" -- both specs use the BASELINE's s^2, neutralising the channel
   920	    The dof correction (N - k) is what makes E[s^2] spec-invariant under the
   921	    null: with RSS/N a richer spec gets a mechanically smaller s^2 even when its
   922	    extra regressor is pure noise.
   923	    """
   924	    if not set(SPECS[base]) < set(SPECS[alt]):
   925	        raise ValueError(
   926	            f"GW requires strict nesting: {base} must be a proper subset of {alt}"
   927	        )
   928	    if smearing not in {"own", "none", "shared"}:
   929	        raise ValueError(f"unknown smearing mode: {smearing}")
   930	
   931	    burn_in = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW if train_window is None else train_window
   932	    cols = list(dict.fromkeys([*SPECS[alt], "y", "y_end_date"]))
   933	    d = p.df.dropna(subset=cols)
   934	    if len(d) < burn_in + GW_MIN_LOSSES:
   935	        return PairedOOS(np.array([]), np.array([]), np.array([]), pd.DatetimeIndex([]))
   936	
   937	    Xb = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS[base]].to_numpy(float)])
   938	    Xa = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS[alt]].to_numpy(float)])
   939	    yraw = d["y"].to_numpy(float)
   940	    yfit = np.log(np.clip(yraw, VAR_FLOOR, None))
   941	    origins = d.index
   942	    y_end = d["y_end_date"].to_numpy()
   943	
   944	    if not pd.Series(y_end).is_monotonic_increasing:
   945	        raise AssertionError(f"[{p.asset}] y_end_date is not monotonic; searchsorted invalid")
   946	
   947	    n_clip = 0
   948	
   949	    def fit(x_all, start, end, i, s2_override=None):
   950	        nonlocal n_clip
   951	        xtr, ytr = x_all[start:end], yfit[start:end]
   952	        # Normal equations, not SVD. The power simulation re-runs this loop
   953	        # ~16k times, and an SVD per origin makes that a half-hour job. There is
   954	        # exactly ONE estimation path in this file -- the simulation must not be
   955	        # allowed to quietly use a different estimator than the headline result.
   956	        # `test_normal_equations_match_lstsq` pins this to the SVD solution.
   957	        gram = xtr.T @ xtr
   958	        try:
   959	            beta = np.linalg.solve(gram, xtr.T @ ytr)
   960	        except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
   961	            beta = np.linalg.lstsq(xtr, ytr, rcond=None)[0]
   962	        resid = ytr - xtr @ beta
   963	        # dof-corrected: E[s2] is then spec-invariant under the null
   964	        s2 = float(resid @ resid / max(len(ytr) - x_all.shape[1], 1))
   965	        mu = float(x_all[i] @ beta)
   966	        # Trap runaway extrapolation BEFORE exponentiating: a regressor far
   967	        # outside its training range can otherwise produce an astronomical
   968	        # variance forecast and let two origins dominate the whole QLIKE mean.
   969	        # Bounds come from training values only and are applied identically to
   970	        # both specs, so neither is advantaged. `n_clipped` reports how often it
   971	        # binds -- if that is 0, this is a pure no-op safety net.
   972	        lo, hi = float(ytr.min()) - 1.0, float(ytr.max()) + 1.0
   973	        if not (lo <= mu <= hi):
   974	            n_clip += 1
   975	            mu = min(max(mu, lo), hi)
   976	        use = s2 if s2_override is None else s2_override
   977	        if smearing == "none":
   978	            return math.exp(mu), s2
   979	        return max(math.exp(mu + 0.5 * use), VAR_FLOOR), s2
   980	
   981	    act, fb, fa, dates, sizes, gaps = [], [], [], [], [], []
   982	    for i in range(burn_in, len(d)):
   983	        # Forward-label embargo: a training row is usable only once its whole
   984	        # label window has closed strictly before this origin.
   985	        end = int(np.searchsorted(y_end, origins[i].to_datetime64(), side="left"))
   986	        end = min(end, i)
   987	        start = 0 if train_window is None else end - train_window
   988	        if start < 0 or end - start < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
   989	            continue
   990	        pb, s2b = fit(Xb, start, end, i)
   991	        # "shared" forces the BASELINE's s^2 onto the augmented spec too
   992	        pa, _ = fit(Xa, start, end, i, s2_override=s2b if smearing == "shared" else None)
   993	        act.append(float(yraw[i]))
   994	        fb.append(pb)
   995	        fa.append(pa)
   996	        dates.append(origins[i])
   997	        sizes.append(end - start)
   998	        gaps.append((origins[i] - pd.Timestamp(y_end[end - 1])).days)
   999	
  1000	    audit = {
  1001	        "scheme": "expanding" if train_window is None else "fixed_rolling",
  1002	        "train_window": None if train_window is None else int(train_window),
  1003	        "n_origins": len(act),
  1004	        "fixed_window_held": bool(
  1005	            train_window is not None
  1006	            and sizes
  1007	            and min(sizes) == max(sizes) == train_window
  1008	        ),
  1009	        "same_training_dates_for_both_models": True,
  1010	        "min_origin_minus_last_train_label_end_days": int(min(gaps)) if gaps else None,
  1011	        "embargo_ok": bool(gaps and min(gaps) >= 1),
  1012	        "smearing": smearing,
  1013	        "n_forecasts_clipped_to_training_range": n_clip,
  1014	    }
  1015	    return PairedOOS(
  1016	        np.array(act), np.array(fb), np.array(fa), pd.DatetimeIndex(dates), audit
  1017	    )
  1018	
  1019	
  1020	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1021	# 8. Inference
  1022	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1023	def _bartlett_lrv(x: np.ndarray, max_lag: int) -> float:
  1024	    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
  1025	    x = x[np.isfinite(x)]
  1026	    n = len(x)
  1027	    if n < 10:
  1028	        raise ValueError("long-run variance needs >=10 finite observations")
  1029	    c = x - float(np.mean(x))
  1030	    lrv = float(c @ c / n)
  1031	    for lag in range(1, min(max_lag, n - 1) + 1):
  1032	        w = 1.0 - lag / (max_lag + 1.0)
  1033	        lrv += 2.0 * w * float(c[lag:] @ c[:-lag] / n)
  1034	    if not np.isfinite(lrv) or lrv <= 0:
  1035	        raise ValueError(f"non-positive long-run variance: {lrv}")
  1036	    return lrv
  1037	
  1038	
  1039	def giacomini_white(loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int) -> dict:
  1040	    """GW (2006) equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test under general loss.
  1041	
  1042	    READ THIS BEFORE CONCLUDING WE JUST RENAMED DM.
  1043	    The arithmetic here -- mean loss difference over a Bartlett HAC standard
  1044	    error -- is algebraically the same as a HAC Diebold-Mariano t. On the same
  1045	    loss stream the two statistics COINCIDE, and the results file shows them
  1046	    coinciding. That is not a bug and it is not a relabelling.
  1047	
  1048	    What makes this a legal test under nesting is the ESTIMATION SCHEME, not the
  1049	    formula. GW tests equal predictive ability of forecasting METHODS -- the
  1050	    fitted-parameter noise is part of the object being compared, not a nuisance
  1051	    to be purged. For that limiting experiment to hold, the estimator must have
  1052	    BOUNDED MEMORY, i.e. a fixed-length rolling window. Feed it fixed-window
  1053	    forecasts and the statistic is asymptotically standard normal even when the
  1054	    models are nested. Feed it EXPANDING-window forecasts -- what v1 did -- and
  1055	    the nested null is degenerate, the statistic is biased toward the smaller
  1056	    model, and no reference distribution rescues it.
  1057	
  1058	    So: same number, different scheme, different null, different validity. The
  1059	    expanding-window value is reported alongside as a diagnostic precisely so a
  1060	    reader can see what the scheme change did.
  1061	
  1062	    Moment: E[L_aug - L_base] = 0.  Negative z favours the flow model.
  1063	    """
  1064	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
  1065	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
  1066	    if aug.shape != base.shape:
  1067	        raise ValueError("loss arrays must have identical shapes")
  1068	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
  1069	    diff = aug[fin] - base[fin]
  1070	    n = len(diff)
  1071	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1072	        raise ValueError(f"GW requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1073	    bw = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
  1074	    lrv = _bartlett_lrv(diff, bw)
  1075	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
  1076	    z = float(np.mean(diff)) / se
  1077	    return {
  1078	        "test": "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability",
  1079	        "loss": "Patton QLIKE",
  1080	        "estimand": "E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base]",
  1081	        "forecast_scheme": "paired fixed rolling estimation window",
  1082	        "null": "equal expected QLIKE loss for the two forecasting METHODS",
  1083	        "direction": "negative z favours the flow model",
  1084	        "n": int(n),
  1085	        "mean_loss_diff_aug_minus_base": float(np.mean(diff)),
  1086	        "z_stat": float(z),
  1087	        "p_value_two_sided": float(2.0 * stats.norm.sf(abs(z))),
  1088	        "p_value_one_sided_flow_better": float(stats.norm.cdf(z)),
  1089	        "hac_lag_used": int(bw),
  1090	        "standard_error": float(se),
  1091	    }
  1092	
  1093	
  1094	def material_gain_exclusion(
  1095	    loss_aug: np.ndarray,
  1096	    loss_base: np.ndarray,
  1097	    h: int,
  1098	    margin: float = MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN,
  1099	) -> dict:
  1100	    """Can a pre-specified relative QLIKE gain be RULED OUT? (C2b)
  1101	
  1102	    This is the only test in the file that can license a bounded null, and it is
  1103	    the one v1 never ran.
  1104	
  1105	    H0: the flow method improves expected QLIKE by at least `margin`,
  1106	        E[L_aug] <= (1 - margin) * E[L_base].
  1107	    Rejecting H0 (positive z, small p) means a gain that large is NOT there.
  1108	
  1109	    Note what this does and does not buy. It reverses the burden of proof, so a
  1110	    rejection is a genuine upper bound on the effect -- but the bound lives in
  1111	    QLIKE-LOSS space ("no >=1% relative QLIKE improvement"), NOT in RV-uplift
  1112	    space. v1's "we can rule out a >=16% RV rise per 1-sd shock" came from
  1113	    reading a power curve backwards, which is not an inference at all. Failing to
  1114	    reject equal accuracy never proves equality; only this does, and only for
  1115	    what it actually measures.
  1116	    """
  1117	    if not 0 < margin < 1:
  1118	        raise ValueError("margin must lie strictly in (0, 1)")
  1119	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
  1120	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
  1121	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
  1122	    moment = aug[fin] - (1.0 - margin) * base[fin]
  1123	    n = len(moment)
  1124	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1125	        raise ValueError(f"exclusion test requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1126	    bw = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
  1127	    lrv = _bartlett_lrv(moment, bw)
  1128	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
  1129	    z = float(np.mean(moment)) / se
  1130	    return {
  1131	        "test": "one-sided material-gain exclusion (fixed-window GW moment)",
  1132	        "margin_relative_qlike": float(margin),
  1133	        "null": f"flow method improves expected QLIKE by at least {100 * margin:.1f}%",
  1134	        "alternative": f"any QLIKE gain is smaller than {100 * margin:.1f}%",
  1135	        "n": int(n),
  1136	        "mean_margin_moment": float(np.mean(moment)),
  1137	        "z_stat": float(z),
  1138	        "p_value_one_sided": float(stats.norm.sf(z)),
  1139	        "hac_lag_used": int(bw),
  1140	        "scope": (
  1141	            "rules out a material QLIKE gain; does NOT prove exact equality, and "
  1142	            "says nothing about RV-uplift magnitude"
  1143	        ),
  1144	    }
  1145	
  1146	
  1147	def qlike_gain_upper_bound(
  1148	    loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int, alpha: float = 0.05
  1149	) -> float | None:
  1150	    """One-sided (1-alpha) UPPER CONFIDENCE BOUND on the relative QLIKE gain, in %.
  1151	
  1152	    This inverts `material_gain_exclusion`: it returns the smallest margin whose
  1153	    one-sided test rejects. Gains larger than the bound are excluded by the data;
  1154	    gains smaller than it are not.
  1155	
  1156	    Why this and not the power curve. Both answer "how big an effect could this
  1157	    design have seen?", but only ONE of them is an inference about the effect. A
  1158	    power curve is a property of the design under an assumed truth; a confidence
  1159	    bound is a statement about the truth given the data. v1 reached for the power
  1160	    curve and read it backwards. This is the object it should have reported.
  1161	
  1162	    Returns None when even a 90% gain cannot be excluded, i.e. the design says
  1163	    essentially nothing about the effect size.
  1164	    """
  1165	    z_crit = float(stats.norm.ppf(1 - alpha))
  1166	
  1167	    def z_of(m: float) -> float:
  1168	        return material_gain_exclusion(loss_aug, loss_base, h, margin=m)["z_stat"]
  1169	
  1170	    lo, hi = 1e-4, 0.90
  1171	    if z_of(hi) < z_crit:
  1172	        return None
  1173	    if z_of(lo) >= z_crit:
  1174	        return lo * 100.0
  1175	    for _ in range(50):                       # z(m) is increasing in m
  1176	        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
  1177	        if z_of(mid) >= z_crit:
  1178	            hi = mid
  1179	        else:
  1180	            lo = mid
  1181	    return hi * 100.0
  1182	
  1183	
  1184	def block_bootstrap_ci(
  1185	    loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int, seed: int, reps: int = 1999
  1186	) -> dict:
  1187	    """Paired circular moving-block CI for the relative QLIKE improvement."""
  1188	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
  1189	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
  1190	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
  1191	    aug, base = aug[fin], base[fin]
  1192	    n = len(aug)
  1193	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1194	        raise ValueError(f"bootstrap requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1195	    bl = max(int(h), int(math.ceil(n ** (1 / 3))))
  1196	    nb = int(math.ceil(n / bl))
  1197	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
  1198	    off = np.arange(bl)
  1199	    draws = np.empty(reps)
  1200	    for b in range(reps):
  1201	        starts = rng.integers(0, n, size=nb)
  1202	        idx = ((starts[:, None] + off[None, :]) % n).ravel()[:n]
  1203	        bm = float(np.mean(base[idx]))
  1204	        draws[b] = 100.0 * (bm - float(np.mean(aug[idx]))) / bm
  1205	    pb = float(np.mean(base))
  1206	    return {
  1207	        "method": "paired circular moving-block bootstrap of fixed-window QLIKE losses",
  1208	        "seed": int(seed),
  1209	        "reps": int(reps),
  1210	        "block_length": int(bl),
  1211	        "point_improvement_pct": 100.0 * (pb - float(np.mean(aug))) / pb,
  1212	        "ci95_improvement_pct": [float(x) for x in np.quantile(draws, [0.025, 0.975])],
  1213	        "scope": "uncertainty diagnostic; forecasts are not re-estimated inside the bootstrap",
  1214	    }
  1215	
  1216	
  1217	def raw_dm_diagnostic(
  1218	    loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int, scheme: str = "fixed"
  1219	) -> dict:
  1220	    """Ordinary Diebold-Mariano on the QLIKE loss difference. DESCRIPTIVE ONLY.
  1221	
  1222	    `scheme` decides WHY it is descriptive, and the two reasons are not the same
  1223	    reason. Writing one label for both is how a file ends up contradicting
  1224	    itself.
  1225	
  1226	      scheme="expanding"  The statistic is INVALID for this comparison. An
  1227	                          expanding window drives estimation error to zero, the
  1228	                          nested null degenerates, and the statistic is biased
  1229	                          toward the smaller model. It is kept only to show what
  1230	                          v1's design produced.
  1231	
  1232	      scheme="fixed"      The statistic is VALID and is simply not the object the
  1233	                          verdict was pre-registered on. It runs on the very loss
  1234	                          stream the Giacomini-White gate runs on -- a fixed
  1235	                          rolling window, so estimation error survives in the
  1236	                          limit and there is NO bias toward the smaller model
  1237	                          here. It differs from the gate statistic only in the
  1238	                          HAC small-sample scaling and in taking a Student-t
  1239	                          rather than a normal reference, which is why the two
  1240	                          agree to ~3 decimals. Calling this one "biased toward
  1241	                          the smaller model" would be importing the expanding-
  1242	                          window pathology into a design that was changed
  1243	                          specifically to avoid it.
  1244	
  1245	    Either way `feeds_gate=False`: exactly one object reaches the verdict, and it
  1246	    is the one whose reference distribution was justified in advance.
  1247	
  1248	    C5 -- naming. `volpred.stats.model_evaluation.dm_test` is a HAC-DM statistic
  1249	    combined with the Harvey-Liu-Zhu (2016) |t| > 3 heuristic threshold. It does
  1250	    NOT apply the Harvey-Leybourne-Newbold (1997) finite-sample correction
  1251	    factor, so v1 was wrong to call it "HLN modified DM". We report it under its
  1252	    true name. The one-sided p-value is taken from the SAME Student-t(n-1)
  1253	    distribution the helper uses for its two-sided p -- v1 switched to a normal
  1254	    CDF here, so its two p-values disagreed with each other.
  1255	    """
  1256	    if scheme not in ("fixed", "expanding"):
  1257	        raise ValueError(f"scheme must be 'fixed' or 'expanding'; got {scheme!r}")
  1258	    t, p_two = dm_test(loss_aug, loss_base, h=h)
  1259	    n = int(np.isfinite(np.asarray(loss_aug) - np.asarray(loss_base)).sum())
  1260	    role = {
  1261	        "fixed": (
  1262	            "diagnostic-only BY DESIGN, not by invalidity: same estimand and same "
  1263	            "fixed-rolling-window loss stream as the Giacomini-White gate, "
  1264	            "differing only in the HAC small-sample scaling and a Student-t "
  1265	            "reference. A fixed window keeps estimation error in the limiting "
  1266	            "experiment, so this statistic is NOT biased toward the smaller model. "
  1267	            "It does not feed the gate because the verdict was pre-registered on "
  1268	            "the object with the justified reference distribution."
  1269	        ),
  1270	        "expanding": (
  1271	            "diagnostic-only BECAUSE INVALID: an expanding window drives estimation "
  1272	            "error to zero, the nested null is degenerate, and the statistic is "
  1273	            "biased toward the smaller model. Reported only to show what v1's "
  1274	            "design produced."
  1275	        ),
  1276	    }[scheme]
  1277	    return {
  1278	        "statistic": "HAC-DM (canonical bandwidth) + Harvey-Liu-Zhu |t|>3 heuristic",
  1279	        "estimation_scheme": scheme,
  1280	        "not_hln": (
  1281	            "This is NOT Harvey-Leybourne-Newbold modified DM: no finite-sample "
  1282	            "correction factor is applied. v1 mislabelled it."
  1283	        ),
  1284	        "role": role,
  1285	        "valid_for_this_nested_comparison": scheme == "fixed",
  1286	        "feeds_gate": False,
  1287	        "t_stat": float(t),
  1288	        "p_two_sided_student_t": float(p_two),
  1289	        "p_one_sided_flow_better_student_t": float(stats.t.cdf(t, df=max(n - 1, 1))),
  1290	        "df": n - 1,
  1291	    }
  1292	
  1293

  1304	    variant: str = "",
  1305	    register_gate: bool = True,

  1290	        "df": n - 1,
  1291	    }
