  1294	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1295	# 9. The cell — one nested comparison, fully inferred
  1296	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1297	def evaluate_cell(
  1298	    p: Panel,
  1299	    base: str,
  1300	    alt: str,
  1301	    family: str,
  1302	    train_window: int = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
  1303	    smearing: str = "own",
  1304	    variant: str = "",
  1305	    register_gate: bool = True,
  1306	    bounded_memory: bool = True,
  1307	) -> dict | None:
  1308	    """Run one nested comparison end to end and register its tests.
  1309	
  1310	    `variant` disambiguates cells that share an (asset, horizon, spec) signature
  1311	    but not a panel -- the four shock-threshold dummies, for instance. The cell
  1312	    key MUST be globally unique: it is the join key between the registry, the
  1313	    loss cache and the verdict, and a silent collision would let a robustness
  1314	    cell's exclusion statistics be wired into the primary family. That is not
  1315	    hypothetical -- it happened, and it is why `_assert_unique_cell` below now
  1316	    fails loudly instead of letting the last writer win.
  1317	
  1318	    `bounded_memory=False` marks a cell whose forecasting METHOD does not satisfy
  1319	    GW's bounded-estimator-memory condition (see `TestRecord`). Such a cell is
  1320	    still registered and still corrected for -- dropping a test after seeing its
  1321	    result would be selection -- but it is labelled, and the family-wise number is
  1322	    reported with and without it.
  1323	    """
  1324	    po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window, smearing)
  1325	    if len(po.actual) < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1326	        return None
  1327	
  1328	    lb = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_base)
  1329	    la = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_aug)
  1330	    h = p.horizon
  1331	    qb, qa = float(np.mean(lb)), float(np.mean(la))
  1332	    improve = (qb - qa) / qb * 100.0
  1333	
  1334	    gw = giacomini_white(la, lb, h)
  1335	    excl = material_gain_exclusion(la, lb, h)
  1336	    excl["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=0.05)
  1337	    excl["upper_bound_note"] = (
  1338	        "One-sided 95% upper confidence bound on the RELATIVE QLIKE gain, obtained "
  1339	        "by inverting the exclusion test. Gains above it are excluded by the data; "
  1340	        "gains below it are not. None = even a 90% gain cannot be excluded."
  1341	    )
  1342	    boot = block_bootstrap_ci(la, lb, h, seed=SEED + h)
  1343	    dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h, scheme="fixed")
  1344	
  1345	    # v1's design, re-run purely so the results file can show what changed. An
  1346	    # expanding window breaks GW's bounded-memory condition, so a HAC t-test on
  1347	    # these losses is NOT a valid nested test -- whatever it happens to say.
  1348	    exp_po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window=None, smearing=smearing)
  1349	    expanding = None
  1350	    if len(exp_po.actual) >= GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1351	        exp_lb = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_base)
  1352	        exp_la = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_aug)
  1353	        exp_dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(exp_la, exp_lb, h, scheme="expanding")
  1354	        expanding = {
  1355	            "scheme": "expanding window (v1's design)",
  1356	            "valid_for_nested_inference": False,
  1357	            "why_invalid": (
  1358	                "GW's limiting experiment needs bounded estimator memory. An "
  1359	                "expanding window has none, so the nested null is degenerate and "
  1360	                "the statistic is biased toward the smaller model."
  1361	            ),
  1362	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1363	            "n_oos": int(len(exp_po.actual)),
  1364	            "qlike_improvement_pct": float(
  1365	                (np.mean(exp_lb) - np.mean(exp_la)) / np.mean(exp_lb) * 100.0
  1366	            ),
  1367	            "hac_t_stat": exp_dm["t_stat"],
  1368	        }
  1369	
  1370	    # Clark-West: a DIFFERENT estimand. CW adjusts the MSPE of variance-level
  1371	    # forecasts; it is not a QLIKE general-loss test and is not relabelled as one.
  1372	    cw = clark_west_test(po.actual, po.pred_base, po.pred_aug, h=h)
  1373	
  1374	    cell = _assert_unique_cell(family, p, alt, smearing, train_window, variant)
  1375	    LOSS_CACHE[cell] = (la, lb, h)
  1376	    register(
  1377	        TestRecord(
  1378	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1379	            inference="giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window",
  1380	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base]",
  1381	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1382	            stat=gw["z_stat"], p_one_sided=gw["p_value_one_sided_flow_better"],
  1383	            feeds_gate=register_gate, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1384	            bounded_memory=bounded_memory,
  1385	        )
  1386	    )
  1387	    register(
  1388	        TestRecord(
  1389	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1390	            inference="raw_dm_qlike",
  1391	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base] (same stream as GW; Student-t reference)",
  1392	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1393	            stat=dm["t_stat"], p_one_sided=dm["p_one_sided_flow_better_student_t"],
  1394	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1395	            bounded_memory=bounded_memory,
  1396	        )
  1397	    )
  1398	    register(
  1399	        TestRecord(
  1400	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1401	            inference="clark_west_mspe",
  1402	            estimand="MSPE of variance-level forecasts (NOT QLIKE)",
  1403	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1404	            stat=float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1405	            p_one_sided=float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1406	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1407	            bounded_memory=bounded_memory,
  1408	        )
  1409	    )
  1410	
  1411	    return {
  1412	        "asset": p.asset,
  1413	        "horizon": h,
  1414	        "rv_proxy": p.rv_col,
  1415	        "base": base,
  1416	        "alt": alt,
  1417	        "cell": cell,
  1418	        "family": family,
  1419	        "state_lag": p.state_lag,
  1420	        "flow_lag": p.flow_lag,
  1421	        "smearing": smearing,
  1422	        "bounded_memory": bounded_memory,
  1423	        "n_oos": int(len(po.actual)),
  1424	        "oos_start": str(po.origins.min().date()),
  1425	        "oos_end": str(po.origins.max().date()),
  1426	        "qlike_base": qb,
  1427	        "qlike_alt": qa,
  1428	        "qlike_improvement_pct": improve,
  1429	        "oos_audit": po.audit,
  1430	        "primary_inference_gw_qlike": gw,
  1431	        "material_gain_exclusion": excl,
  1432	        "bootstrap_ci": boot,
  1433	        "raw_dm_diagnostic": dm,
  1434	        "expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design": expanding,
  1435	        "statistic_coincidence_note": (
  1436	            "raw_dm_diagnostic.t_stat and primary_inference_gw_qlike.z_stat are the "
  1437	            "same statistic up to a small-sample HAC scaling (the GW long-run "
  1438	            "variance divides each lag covariance by n, the canonical DM helper by "
  1439	            "n-lag), and they are computed on the SAME fixed-window loss stream. "
  1440	            "They therefore agree to ~3 decimal places. That is expected, not a "
  1441	            "relabelling: what separates a valid nested test from an invalid one "
  1442	            "here is the ESTIMATION SCHEME and the null, not the arithmetic. The "
  1443	            "raw statistic is nevertheless tagged feeds_gate=false so that the "
  1444	            "verdict is carried by the object whose reference distribution is "
  1445	            "justified. Compare with expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design to see "
  1446	            "what the scheme change actually did."
  1447	        ),
  1448	        "clark_west_mspe_separate_estimand": {
  1449	            "t_stat": float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1450	            "p_one_sided": float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1451	            "estimand": "MSPE (variance level), not QLIKE",
  1452	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1453	            "note": (
  1454	                "Clark-West corrects the MSPE of the nested comparison. It is "
  1455	                "evidence about a different loss than the one the verdict uses, "
  1456	                "and is reported as such."
  1457	            ),
  1458	        },
  1459	    }

  1465	def fit_calendar_har(rv: pd.DataFrame, rv_col: str) -> dict:

  1470	    including the weekends when no flow prints, so it needs a calendar-day law of
  1471	    motion.
  1472	    """
  1473	    lr = np.log(rv[rv_col])
  1474	    frame = pd.DataFrame(

  1462	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1463	# 10. Power simulation (C2a) — a statement about the DESIGN, not the effect
  1464	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1465	def fit_calendar_har(rv: pd.DataFrame, rv_col: str) -> dict:
  1466	    """Fit the HAR+ctrl recursion on CALENDAR days. This is the simulation DGP.
  1467	
  1468	    Distinct from `in_sample`, which fits on PANEL rows (flow days only). The
  1469	    simulation has to step the volatility process forward on every calendar day,
  1470	    including the weekends when no flow prints, so it needs a calendar-day law of
  1471	    motion.
  1472	    """
  1473	    lr = np.log(rv[rv_col])
  1474	    frame = pd.DataFrame(
  1475	        {
  1476	            "y": lr,
  1477	            "har_d": lr.shift(1),
  1478	            "har_w": lr.rolling(HAR_W).mean().shift(1),
  1479	            "har_m": lr.rolling(HAR_M).mean().shift(1),
  1480	            "ret": rv["ret"].shift(1),
  1481	            "abs_ret": rv["ret"].abs().shift(1),
  1482	        }
  1483	    ).dropna()
  1484	    cols = ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"]
  1485	    X = frame[cols].to_numpy(float)
  1486	    y = frame["y"].to_numpy(float)
  1487	    beta = _ols(X, y)
  1488	    resid = y - np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]) @ beta
  1489	    persistence = float(beta[1] + beta[2] + beta[3])
  1490	    if persistence >= 1.0:
  1491	        raise AssertionError(
  1492	            f"calendar HAR is non-stationary (phi_d+phi_w+phi_m = {persistence:.4f}); "
  1493	            "the simulated paths would explode"
  1494	        )
  1495	    return {
  1496	        "beta": beta,
  1497	        "resid": resid,
  1498	        "n": int(len(frame)),
  1499	        "persistence": persistence,
  1500	        "resid_sd": float(np.std(resid)),
  1501	    }
  1502	
  1503	
  1504	def _block_resample(resid: np.ndarray, n_out: int, rng, block: int) -> np.ndarray:
  1505	    """Circular moving-block resample: keeps the innovation autocorrelation."""
  1506	    nb = int(math.ceil(n_out / block))
  1507	    starts = rng.integers(0, len(resid), size=nb)
  1508	    idx = ((starts[:, None] + np.arange(block)[None, :]) % len(resid)).ravel()[:n_out]
  1509	    return resid[idx]
  1510	
  1511	
  1512	def simulate_lrv(
  1513	    lr_seed: np.ndarray,
  1514	    ret_lag: np.ndarray,
  1515	    aret_lag: np.ndarray,
  1516	    shock: np.ndarray,
  1517	    dgp: dict,
  1518	    beta_flow: float,
  1519	    rng,
  1520	    bounds: tuple[float, float],
  1521	) -> tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
  1522	    """One simulated calendar log-RV path carrying a KNOWN flow effect.
  1523	
  1524	    The effect is injected into the LAW OF MOTION, so it propagates into
  1525	    tomorrow's HAR features exactly as a genuine effect would. That matters: the
  1526	    HAR baseline partially absorbs a real effect through its own lags, which
  1527	    makes the effect HARDER to detect. Injecting into the target only -- without
  1528	    propagation -- would overstate power, which is the optimistic direction and
  1529	    therefore the dangerous one.
  1530	    """
  1531	    b = dgp["beta"]
  1532	    n = len(shock)
  1533	    lrv = np.empty(n)
  1534	    lrv[:HAR_M] = lr_seed[:HAR_M]
  1535	    e = _block_resample(dgp["resid"], n, rng, block=max(2, int(math.ceil(n ** (1 / 3)))))
  1536	    lo, hi = bounds
  1537	    n_clip = 0
  1538	    sw = float(lrv[HAR_M - HAR_W : HAR_M].sum())
  1539	    sm = float(lrv[:HAR_M].sum())
  1540	    for d in range(HAR_M, n):
  1541	        v = (
  1542	            b[0]
  1543	            + b[1] * lrv[d - 1]
  1544	            + b[2] * (sw / HAR_W)
  1545	            + b[3] * (sm / HAR_M)
  1546	            + b[4] * ret_lag[d]
  1547	            + b[5] * aret_lag[d]
  1548	            + beta_flow * shock[d]
  1549	            + e[d]
  1550	        )
  1551	        if not (lo <= v <= hi):
  1552	            n_clip += 1
  1553	            v = min(max(v, lo), hi)
  1554	        lrv[d] = v
  1555	        sw += lrv[d] - lrv[d - HAR_W]
  1556	        sm += lrv[d] - lrv[d - HAR_M]
  1557	    return lrv, n_clip
  1558	
  1559	
  1560	def power_simulation(
  1561	    rv: pd.DataFrame,
  1562	    ff: FlowFeatures,
  1563	    asset: str,
  1564	    horizon: int = 1,
  1565	    reps: int = POWER_REPS,
  1566	    betas: tuple[float, ...] = POWER_BETAS,
  1567	) -> dict:
  1568	    """Simulated power of ONE cell's GW gate, plus a false-positive check at beta=0.
  1569	
  1570	    v1's "MDE" was not a power analysis at all: it injected each beta ONCE into
  1571	    the single realised noise path and took the first crossing. No repeated
  1572	    sampling, no false-positive check, no confidence interval -- and the resulting
  1573	    curve was not even monotone in beta, which is a tell that it was reading
  1574	    noise. It then INVERTED the logic and claimed the null "excludes effects
  1575	    >= the MDE". Power cannot do that. Only the exclusion test can, and it is
  1576	    reported separately.
  1577	
  1578	    SCOPE -- what this is NOT (quote it with these limits or not at all):
  1579	      * It is the power of a SINGLE CELL against the NOMINAL one-sided 5% gate
  1580	        (z < -1.645). The verdict runs a ten-cell Holm-corrected family, whose
  1581	        power is strictly LOWER. This is not "the power of the study".
  1582	      * `horizon` defaults to 1. The primary family also contains h = 5 cells,
  1583	        which are not simulated here.
  1584	      * The injected effect is a single |flow| shock entering the HAR law of
  1585	        motion -- the H1-style alternative. The H2 asymmetry and the cross-asset
  1586	        H4 alternative are NOT simulated, so this curve says nothing about the
  1587	        design's power against THEM.
  1588	      * The beta = 0 row is a FALSE-POSITIVE DIAGNOSTIC, not a size calibration:
  1589	        under GW's method-level null with a fixed window, an irrelevant regressor
  1590	        makes the augmented method genuinely worse, so the flow-favouring
  1591	        one-sided test is CONSERVATIVE at beta = 0 by construction. A rate below
  1592	        5% there is the correct behaviour, not evidence of a calibrated size.
  1593	
  1594	    What this function establishes: for an effect of size beta, how often would
  1595	    that one gate fire? Nothing more.
  1596	    """
  1597	    dgp = fit_calendar_har(rv, "rv_gk")
  1598	    idx = rv.index
  1599	    lr_real = np.log(rv["rv_gk"])
  1600	    lr_seed = lr_real.ffill().bfill().to_numpy()
  1601	    missing = rv["rv_gk"].isna().to_numpy()
  1602	
  1603	    z_cal = ff.z.reindex(idx)
  1604	    shock = z_cal.abs().shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()     # |z_{d-1}|, as in H1
  1605	    ret_lag = rv["ret"].shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
  1606	    aret_lag = rv["ret"].abs().shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
  1607	
  1608	    # Keep simulated log-variance inside a generous envelope of what the asset has
  1609	    # actually done. Without it a draw from the tail of the innovation block can
  1610	    # walk a persistent HAR into absurd territory and one path dominates the
  1611	    # rejection rate. `clip_rate` reports how often it binds.
  1612	    obs = lr_real.dropna()
  1613	    bounds = (float(obs.min()) - 3.0, float(obs.max()) + 3.0)
  1614	
  1615	    rows = []
  1616	    for b in betas:
  1617	        rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED + int(round(b * 10_000)))
  1618	        zs, clips, fails = [], 0, 0
  1619	        for _ in range(reps):
  1620	            lrv, nc = simulate_lrv(
  1621	                lr_seed, ret_lag, aret_lag, shock, dgp, b, rng, bounds
  1622	            )
  1623	            clips += nc
  1624	            sim = rv[["ret"]].copy()
  1625	            sim["rv_gk"] = np.exp(lrv)
  1626	            sim.loc[missing, "rv_gk"] = np.nan     # preserve the real missingness
  1627	            try:
  1628	                p = build_panel(sim, ff, "rv_gk", horizon, asset)
  1629	                po = paired_oos(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
  1630	                if len(po.actual) < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1631	                    fails += 1
  1632	                    continue
  1633	                gw = giacomini_white(
  1634	                    qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_aug),
  1635	                    qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_base),
  1636	                    horizon,
  1637	                )
  1638	                zs.append(gw["z_stat"])
  1639	            except (ValueError, AssertionError):
  1640	                fails += 1
  1641	        z = np.asarray(zs)
  1642	        n_ok = len(z)
  1643	        rej_5 = float(np.mean(z < POWER_GATE_Z)) if n_ok else float("nan")
  1644	        rej_h = float(np.mean(z < -3.0)) if n_ok else float("nan")
  1645	        rows.append(
  1646	            {
  1647	                "beta": b,
  1648	                "rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct": round((math.exp(b) - 1) * 100, 2),
  1649	                "reps_completed": n_ok,
  1650	                "reps_failed": fails,
  1651	                "power_gw_one_sided_5pct": rej_5,
  1652	                "power_gw_se": (
  1653	                    float(math.sqrt(max(rej_5 * (1 - rej_5), 0) / n_ok)) if n_ok else None
  1654	                ),
  1655	                "power_gw_harvey_z_lt_minus3": rej_h,
  1656	                "median_gw_z": float(np.median(z)) if n_ok else None,
  1657	                "clip_rate_per_path": round(clips / max(n_ok, 1) / len(idx), 6),
  1658	            }
  1659	        )
  1660	
  1661	    size = next((r for r in rows if r["beta"] == 0.0), None)
  1662	
  1663	    def _first_at(power: float) -> dict | None:
  1664	        return next((r for r in rows if r["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] >= power), None)
  1665	
  1666	    def _bracket(power: float) -> dict:
  1667	        """The INTERVAL that contains the power crossing. Never a point.
  1668	
  1669	        The grid is coarse (8 betas), so the effect size at which power first
  1670	        reaches a target is only ever bracketed: it lies strictly between the last
  1671	        grid point BELOW the target and the first grid point AT OR ABOVE it. v1's
  1672	        signature error was turning a coarse curve into a precise-sounding number;
  1673	        reporting the upper grid point as "the 80%-power effect" would be a smaller
  1674	        version of the same move, so no point estimate is emitted at all -- only
  1675	        `lower_rv_uplift_pct` / `upper_rv_uplift_pct`, and `upper` is None when the
  1676	        grid never reaches the target (the crossing is then beyond the grid, or
  1677	        does not exist).
  1678	        """
  1679	        hit = _first_at(power)
  1680	        below = [r for r in rows if hit is None or r["beta"] < hit["beta"]]
  1681	        prev = below[-1] if below else None
  1682	        reached = hit is not None
  1683	        return {
  1684	            "target_power": power,
  1685	            "reached_on_grid": reached,
  1686	            "lower_rv_uplift_pct": prev["rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct"] if prev else None,
  1687	            "lower_grid_power": prev["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] if prev else None,
  1688	            "upper_rv_uplift_pct": hit["rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct"] if reached else None,
  1689	            "upper_grid_power": hit["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] if reached else None,
  1690	            "lower_beta": prev["beta"] if prev else None,
  1691	            "upper_beta": hit["beta"] if reached else None,
  1692	            "note": (
  1693	                (
  1694	                    "The crossing lies strictly INSIDE this interval. It is a bracket, "
  1695	                    "not a solved threshold, and must never be quoted as a single "
  1696	                    "number."
  1697	                )
  1698	                if reached
  1699	                else (
  1700	                    f"Power never reaches {power:.0%} anywhere on the grid (the "
  1701	                    f"largest effect simulated is +"
  1702	                    f"{rows[-1]['rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct']}% RV uplift, where power "
  1703	                    f"is only {rows[-1]['power_gw_one_sided_5pct']:.2f}). No bracket "
  1704	                    "exists; the honest statement is 'not reached', not a number."
  1705	                )
  1706	            ),
  1707	        }
  1708	
  1709	    return {
  1710	        "asset": asset,
  1711	        "horizon": horizon,
  1712	        "design": (
  1713	            "Semi-parametric DGP: calendar-day HAR+ctrl law of motion fitted on the "
  1714	            "real series, innovations drawn by circular moving-block bootstrap, real "
  1715	            "flow shocks and real returns retained, effect injected into the law of "
  1716	            "motion so it propagates through the HAR lags."
  1717	        ),
  1718	        "reps_per_beta": reps,
  1719	        "seed": SEED,
  1720	        "gate": (
  1721	            f"SINGLE-CELL nominal GW gate: z < {POWER_GATE_Z} (one-sided 5%), "
  1722	            "pre-specified. This is NOT the ten-cell Holm-corrected gate that "
  1723	            "produces the verdict."
  1724	        ),
  1725	        "scope": {
  1726	            "what_this_is": (
  1727	                "The power of ONE pre-specified cell's gate against a single "
  1728	                "injected alternative, at one horizon."
  1729	            ),
  1730	            "what_this_is_not": (
  1731	                "The power of the study. The verdict is adjudicated on a ten-cell "
  1732	                "family with a Holm correction, which is strictly less powerful than "
  1733	                "the nominal single-cell gate simulated here. No number in this "
  1734	                "object may be quoted as the design's family-wise power."
  1735	            ),
  1736	            "horizon_simulated": horizon,
  1737	            "primary_family_horizons": list(HORIZONS),
  1738	            "horizons_not_simulated": [h for h in HORIZONS if h != horizon],
  1739	            "alternative_simulated": (
  1740	                "a single |flow| z-shock injected into the HAR law of motion "
  1741	                "(the H1-style alternative)"
  1742	            ),
  1743	            "alternatives_not_simulated": [
  1744	                "H2_asym (signed / asymmetric flow response)",
  1745	                "H4_plus_btc (cross-asset BTC flow spillover into ETH)",
  1746	            ],
  1747	            "multiplicity_in_the_simulated_gate": "none (nominal 5%, single cell)",
  1748	            "multiplicity_in_the_actual_verdict": "Holm across the 10-cell family",
  1749	        },
  1750	        "dgp_persistence_phi_sum": round(dgp["persistence"], 4),
  1751	        "dgp_resid_sd": round(dgp["resid_sd"], 4),
  1752	        "false_positive_rate_at_beta_0": size["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] if size else None,
  1753	        "false_positive_note": (
  1754	            "This is the rejection rate of the flow-favouring gate when the true "
  1755	            "effect is ZERO, and it must be read carefully -- it is NOT 'size' in "
  1756	            "the textbook sense. Under GW's method-level null with a fixed window, "
  1757	            "an irrelevant extra regressor makes the augmented METHOD genuinely "
  1758	            "worse: it pays an estimation cost and buys nothing, so "
  1759	            "E[L_aug - L_base] > 0 strictly. The one-sided flow-favouring test is "
  1760	            "therefore CONSERVATIVE at beta = 0 by construction, and a rate well "
  1761	            "below the nominal 5% is the expected, correct behaviour -- not a bug "
  1762	            "and not a calibration failure. What it does establish is the thing we "
  1763	            "need: this gate does not manufacture flow signals out of noise. A rate "
  1764	            "materially ABOVE 5% would have been the alarming result."
  1765	        ),
  1766	        "curve": rows,
  1767	        "power_80pct_bracket": _bracket(0.80),
  1768	        "power_90pct_bracket": _bracket(0.90),
  1769	        "grid_note": (
  1770	            "beta is evaluated on a COARSE grid of 8 points, so the effect size at "
  1771	            "which power first reaches a target is BRACKETED, never solved. The "
  1772	            "brackets above are the only admissible reading: a crossing lies "
  1773	            "somewhere inside [lower_rv_uplift_pct, upper_rv_uplift_pct]. This "
  1774	            "object deliberately emits NO point estimate of an 80%- or 90%-power "
  1775	            "effect size -- an earlier revision printed the upper grid point as if "
  1776	            "it were the threshold, which is the same 'coarse curve, precise number' "
  1777	            "move that got v1 failed, only smaller."
  1778	        ),
  1779	        "max_beta_tested": max(betas),
  1780	        "max_uplift_tested_pct": round((math.exp(max(betas)) - 1) * 100, 2),
  1781	        "scope_warning": (
  1782	            "POWER IS NOT AN EXCLUSION. This says how often the gate fires against "
  1783	            "an effect of a given size. It does NOT say the true effect is smaller "
  1784	            "than the 80%-power point -- that inversion is exactly the error v1 "
  1785	            "made when it claimed to 'rule out >= +16% RV per 1-sd shock'. The only "
  1786	            "upper bound this study can defend is the material-gain exclusion test, "
  1787	            "and it lives in QLIKE-loss space. "
  1788	            "Also note the SCOPE (see `scope`): this is PER-CELL power at the nominal "
  1789	            "5% gate, at h = 1, against ONE injected alternative. The verdict's family "
  1790	            "additionally applies a Holm correction, spans h = 5 as well, and includes "
  1791	            "alternatives that are not simulated here -- so the family-wise design has "
  1792	            "LESS power than the curve below, and the curve says nothing at all about "
  1793	            "power against H2_asym or H4_plus_btc."
  1794	        ),
  1795	    }
  1796

  1837	            "Forecast origin = 00:00 UTC ending calendar day t. Farside publishes "
  1838	            "day-t flows ~21:00 UTC on day t, so flow_t and the completed UTC day-t "
  1839	            "RV are both known; target RV_{t+1..t+h} lies entirely ahead. State "
  1840	            "controls carry state_lag=1 always; only the flow carries flow_lag."
  1841	        ),
  1842	        "inference_design": {
  1843	            "problem": (
  1844	                "HAR+ctrl vs HAR+ctrl+flow is a STRICTLY NESTED comparison of "
  1845	                "forecasting methods. What breaks an ordinary loss-difference "
  1846	                "statistic is nesting COMBINED WITH an estimation scheme whose "
  1847	                "estimation error vanishes: under an expanding (recursive) window the "
  1848	                "two methods' forecasts converge under the null, the loss "
  1849	                "differential degenerates and the statistic tilts toward the smaller "
  1850	                "model. That is v1's design. Under a FIXED ROLLING window the "
  1851	                "estimation error survives in the limit, the loss differential keeps "
  1852	                "a non-degenerate variance, and the comparison is legal again -- "
  1853	                "which is exactly why the scheme was changed, and why it would be "
  1854	                "wrong to keep calling the fixed-window statistic 'biased toward the "
  1855	                "smaller model'."
  1856	            ),
  1857	            "claim_bearing_test": (
  1858	                "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability on "
  1859	                "Patton QLIKE, from a PAIRED FIXED ROLLING WINDOW (both specs share "
  1860	                "the augmented complete-case mask, the training dates, the embargo "

  1862	            ),
  1863	            "bounded_null_test": (

  1969	            {

  1984	        }
  1985	    res["endogeneity_diagnostic"] = endo
  1986	    res["endogeneity_note"] = (

  1995	    panels: dict[tuple, Panel] = {}
  1996	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1997	        for h in HORIZONS:
  1998	            bz = btc_z_cal if a == "ETH" else None
  1999	            p = build_panel(rvs[a], feats[a], "rv_gk", h, a, flow_lag=1, btc_z=bz)
  2000	            assert_no_lookahead(p)
  2001	            panels[(a, h)] = p
  2002	            print(
  2003	                f"  panel {a} h={h}: n={len(p.df)} "
  2004	                f"{p.df.index.min().date()}..{p.df.index.max().date()}"
  2005	            )
  2006	
  2007	    # --- in-sample (descriptive) ---------------------------------------------
  2008	    insample = {}
  2009	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():
  2010	        for spec in ("HAR", "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", "H2_asym"):
  2011	            insample[f"{a}_h{h}_{spec}"] = in_sample(p, spec)
  2012	    res["in_sample"] = insample
  2013	    res["in_sample_note"] = (
  2014	        "Descriptive. The verdict rests on out-of-sample predictive ability, not "
  2015	        "on an in-sample coefficient."
  2016	    )
  2017	
  2018	    # --- PRIMARY family: H1 / H2 on both assets and horizons ------------------
  2019	    cells = []
  2020	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():
  2021	        for alt in ("H1_absflow", "H2_asym"):
  2022	            c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", alt, family="primary")
  2023	            if c:
  2024	                cells.append(c)
  2025	
  2026	    # --- PRIMARY family: BTC flow -> ETH RV spillover -------------------------
  2027	    for h in HORIZONS:

  2031	            state_lag=p.state_lag, flow_lag=p.flow_lag,
  2032	        )
  2033	        c = evaluate_cell(pf, "H4_own", "H4_plus_btc", family="primary")
  2034	        if c:
  2035	            c["description"] = "BTC flow shock -> ETH RV, controlling ETH's own flow"
  2036	            cells.append(c)
  2037	    # A COPY. `= cells` aliased the same list object, so every robustness cell
  2038	    # appended below silently landed in "primary_cells" too.
  2039	    res["primary_cells"] = list(cells)
  2040	    primary_cell_keys = {c["cell"] for c in cells}
  2041	
  2042	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: RV proxy ------------------------------------------------
  2043	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):

  2050	            if c:

  2065	                )
  2066	                cells.append(c)
  2067

  2069	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  2070	        for h in HORIZONS:

  2075	                )
  2076	                if c:
  2077	                    cells.append(c)
  2078	
  2079	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: flow transforms (Warther 1995) ---------------------------
  2080	    CTRL = SPECS["HAR+ctrl"]
  2081	    TRANSFORMS = {
  2082	        "signed_z": "z_signed",
  2083	        "squared_z": "z_sq",

  2085	        "unexpected_z": "abs_z_unexp",
  2086	    }
  2087	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):

  2089	        for label, col in TRANSFORMS.items():
  2090	            if col not in p.df.columns:
  2091	                continue
  2092	            SPECS[f"T_{label}"] = CTRL + [col]
  2093	            pt = Panel(

  2095	                state_lag=p.state_lag, flow_lag=p.flow_lag,
  2096	            )
  2097	            # The AR(5)-unexpected transform is the one cell in the whole study
  2098	            # whose forecasting METHOD lacks bounded estimator memory: the AR(5)

  2101	            # dropping it after seeing its result would be selection -- but it is

  2105	                bounded_memory=(label != "unexpected_z"),

  2112	                        "The AR(5) that builds this regressor refits on an EXPANDING "

  2114	                        "value never enters its own fit), but the forecasting METHOD "

  2120	                    )
  2121	                cells.append(c)
  2122	
  2123	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: shock-threshold dummies ---------------------------------
  2124	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():

  2129	            c = evaluate_cell(

  2133	            if c:

  2135	                c["n_shock_days"] = int((p.df["abs_z"] >= thr).sum())
  2136	                cells.append(c)
  2137	
  2138	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: shorter ETH burn-in --------------------------------------
  2139	    for h in HORIZONS:
  2140	        c = evaluate_cell(
  2141	            panels[("ETH", h)], "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow",
  2142	            family="eth_window", train_window=200,
  2143	        )
  2144	        if c:
  2145	            cells.append(c)
  2146	
  2147	    res["all_cells"] = cells
  2148

  2150	    h3 = {}

  2187	    # --- Multiple testing, derived from the registry (C6) ----------------------
  2188	    gw_all = [
  2189	        r for r in REGISTRY if r.inference == "giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window"
  2190	    ]
  2191	    gw_primary = [r for r in gw_all if r.family == "primary"]
  2192	    if not gw_primary:
  2193	        raise AssertionError("primary family is empty — nothing to adjudicate")
  2194	
  2195	    holm_primary = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_primary])
  2196	    holm_full = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_all])
  2197	
  2198	    by_cell = {c["cell"]: c for c in cells}
  2199	    if len(by_cell) != len(cells):
  2200	        raise AssertionError("cell keys are not unique; the verdict join is unsafe")
  2201	    # every primary GW record must resolve to a cell that really is primary
  2202	    for rec in gw_primary:
  2203	        if rec.cell not in primary_cell_keys:
  2204	            raise AssertionError(
  2205	                f"primary registry record {rec.cell!r} does not resolve to a primary "
  2206	                "cell -- the registry/loss-cache join has drifted"
  2207	            )
  2208	    excl_p = [
  2209	        by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["p_value_one_sided"]
  2210	        for r in gw_primary
  2211	    ]
  2212	    excl_z = [by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["z_stat"] for r in gw_primary]
  2213	    holm_excl = holm(excl_p)
  2214	
  2215	    # The two families need OPPOSITE multiplicity treatments, because the two
  2216	    # claims have opposite logical structure. This asymmetry is not a convenience;
  2217	    # it is what the claims actually require.
  2218	    #
  2219	    #   "flow helps SOMEWHERE"     -> a UNION of alternatives. Ten shots at finding
  2220	    #                                 an effect, so the family-wise error must be
  2221	    #                                 controlled: Holm on the GW p-values.
  2222	    #
  2223	    #   "flow helps NOWHERE by >=m" -> an INTERSECTION of alternatives, i.e. an
  2224	    #                                 intersection-union test (Berger 1982). We may
  2225	    #                                 assert it only if EVERY cell rejects its own
  2226	    #                                 exclusion null. Under any configuration where
  2227	    #                                 the global claim is false, at least one null is
  2228	    #                                 true, and we wrongly reject it with probability
  2229	    #                                 <= alpha. The conjunction therefore holds at
  2230	    #                                 level alpha with NO adjustment -- and adjusting
  2231	    #                                 anyway would inflate type-II error while buying
  2232	    #                                 no type-I protection at all.
  2233	    #
  2234	    # Holm-adjusted exclusion p-values are still reported as a conservative
  2235	    # sensitivity, so the choice is auditable rather than asserted.
  2236	    primary_rows = []
  2237	    for r, hp, hz, hx, praw in zip(
  2238	        gw_primary, holm_primary, excl_z, holm_excl, excl_p
  2239	    ):
  2240	        row = _serialize(r, holm_p=hp)
  2241	        row["passes_flow_gate"] = bool(
  2242	            r.qlike_improve_pct is not None
  2243	            and r.qlike_improve_pct > 0
  2244	            and r.stat < POWER_GATE_Z
  2245	            and hp < 0.05
  2246	        )
  2247	        row["excludes_material_gain"] = bool(hz > 0 and praw < 0.05)   # IU: unadjusted
  2248	        row["material_gain_exclusion_p_raw"] = praw
  2249	        row["material_gain_exclusion_holm_p"] = hx
  2250	        row["excludes_material_gain_holm_conservative"] = bool(hz > 0 and hx < 0.05)
  2251	        ex = by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]
  2252	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = ex["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"]
  2253	        primary_rows.append(row)
  2254	
  2255	    # Simultaneous (Bonferroni) upper bounds: each cell's one-sided bound computed
  2256	    # at alpha/m so the whole family holds jointly at 95%. Conservative on purpose.
  2257	    m = len(gw_primary)
  2258	    simultaneous = [
  2259	        qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(r.cell, alpha=0.05 / m) for r in gw_primary
  2260	    ]
  2261	    for row, b in zip(primary_rows, simultaneous):
  2262	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_simultaneous"] = b
  2263	    finite = [b for b in simultaneous if b is not None]
  2264	    # The family-wide statement is only as strong as its WEAKEST cell: a bound that
  2265	    # holds for 9 cells and fails for the 10th does not hold for the family.
  2266	    family_bound = max(finite) if len(finite) == m else None
  2267	
  2268	    n_pass = int(sum(r["passes_flow_gate"] for r in primary_rows))
  2269	    n_excl = int(sum(r["excludes_material_gain"] for r in primary_rows))
  2270	    n_excl_holm = int(
  2271	        sum(r["excludes_material_gain_holm_conservative"] for r in primary_rows)
  2272	    )
  2273	    all_excl = bool(primary_rows) and all(

  2275	    )
  2276	
  2277	    # Bounded-memory sensitivity. One registered GW cell -- the AR(5)-unexpected
  2278	    # flow transform -- builds its regressor with an expanding-window fit, so its
  2279	    # forecasting method does not satisfy GW's bounded-memory condition. It is NOT
  2280	    # dropped (removing a test once its result is known is selection); it is
  2281	    # flagged, and the family-wise conclusion is re-derived without it so a reader
  2282	    # can see whether anything hangs on it.
  2283	    gw_bm = [r for r in gw_all if r.bounded_memory]
  2284	    holm_bm = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_bm])
  2285	    n_sig_all = int(sum(hp < 0.05 and r.stat < 0 for r, hp in zip(gw_all, holm_full)))
  2286	    n_sig_bm = int(sum(hp < 0.05 and r.stat < 0 for r, hp in zip(gw_bm, holm_bm)))
  2287	
  2288	    res["multiple_testing"] = {

  2292	            "runs on RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."
  2293	        ),

  2302	        "bounded_memory_sensitivity": {

  2354	        claim = (

  2356	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells REJECT the hypothesis that "
  2357	            f"adding ETF flow improves expected QLIKE by at least {margin_pct:.0f}% "
  2358	            f"(intersection-union test, each cell unadjusted; "

  2361	            f"material ({margin_pct:.0f}%+) QLIKE improvement over a HAR-RV baseline, "
  2362	            f"out of sample.' The bound lives in QLIKE-LOSS space. It is NOT a bound "
  2363	            f"on RV-uplift magnitude and NOT a proof of exact zero."

  2365	    elif verdict == "INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM":

  2375	                "sample can say about the effect size."
  2376	            )
  2377	        )

  2393	    res["verdict_basis"] = {

     1	"""K1709-rev regression gates.
     2	
     3	The v1 suite passed 26/26 while the experiment was making two CRITICAL inference
     4	errors, so "the tests are green" was never the issue -- they tested the plumbing
     5	and not the claim. This suite adds the checks the 2026-07-14 independent review
     6	said were missing:
     7	
     8	  * the calendar precondition every `.shift()` silently assumes
     9	    (`test_deleting_a_sunday_is_rejected`)
    10	  * that nothing already computed can be moved by data arriving later
    11	    (`test_future_data_cannot_move_a_past_forecast`)
    12	  * that US market holidays are not counted as genuine zero-flow days
    13	    (`test_session_filter_*`)
    14	  * that the flow lag and the state lag move independently
    15	    (`test_state_lag_and_flow_lag_are_independent`)
    16	  * that raw Diebold-Mariano cannot reach a verdict
    17	    (`test_raw_dm_never_feeds_a_gate`,
    18	     `test_repo_nested_dm_auditor_marks_this_file_safe`)
    19	  * that power is never reported as an exclusion
    20	    (`test_power_is_not_reported_as_an_exclusion`)
    21	
    22	and, after the 2026-07-14 re-review of the frozen rebuild, the residuals it found:
    23	
    24	  * that the power curve admits its own scope -- one cell, h=1, one alternative,
    25	    nominal gate -- and is never quoted as the study's power
    26	    (`test_power_scope_admits_it_is_not_the_studys_power`)
    27	  * that the 80%/90%-power effect is BRACKETED, never a point estimate
    28	    (`test_power_bracket_reports_an_interval_not_a_solved_threshold`)
    29	  * that beta=0 is a false-positive check, not a size calibration
    30	    (`test_beta0_row_is_a_false_positive_check_not_a_size_calibration`)
    31	  * that the FIXED-window raw statistic is not smeared with the expanding
    32	    window's pathology (`test_fixed_window_raw_dm_is_not_labelled_biased`)
    33	  * that the one cell without bounded estimator memory is labelled, not pooled
    34	    (`test_unbounded_memory_cell_is_flagged_not_silently_pooled`)
    35	
    36	  nested-dm: diagnostic-only  -- this suite calls `raw_dm_diagnostic` only to pin
    37	  down its LABELS and to assert it can never reach a verdict. No raw
    38	  Diebold-Mariano statistic is used as inference anywhere in it.
    39	
    40	Run:  uv run --extra dev python -m pytest experiments/k1709/test_k1709.py -q
    41	"""

    57	from k1709 import (  # noqa: E402

    89	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    90	# Synthetic world — a complete 24/7 RV calendar, flows only on NYSE sessions
    91	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92	def _make_world(
    93	    n_days: int = 900,
    94	    start: str = "2023-01-02",
    95	    seed: int = SEED,
    96	    flow_beta: float = 0.0,
    97	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame]:
    98	    """`flow_beta` injects a KNOWN |z| effect into log RV, so the same helper
    99	    drives both the placebo tests (beta = 0) and the power tests (beta large)."""
   100	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   101	    cal = pd.date_range(start, periods=n_days, freq="D")
   102	    sessions = us_equity_sessions(cal.min(), cal.max())
   103	    fdays = cal.intersection(sessions)
   104	
   105	    flow = pd.Series(rng.normal(0, 300, len(fdays)), index=fdays)
   106	    shock = flow_zscore(flow).abs().reindex(cal).shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
   107	
   108	    lrv = np.empty(n_days)
   109	    lrv[:HAR_M] = -9.0 + rng.normal(0, 0.3, HAR_M)
   110	    e = rng.normal(0, 0.45, n_days)
   111	    for d in range(HAR_M, n_days):
   112	        lrv[d] = (
   113	            -9.0 * 0.15
   114	            + 0.40 * lrv[d - 1]
   115	            + 0.30 * lrv[d - 5 : d].mean()
   116	            + 0.15 * lrv[d - HAR_M : d].mean()
   117	            + flow_beta * shock[d]
   118	            + e[d]
   119	        )
   120	    rv = pd.DataFrame(
   121	        {"rv_gk": np.exp(lrv), "ret": rng.normal(0, 0.03, n_days)}, index=cal
   122	    )
   123	    flow_df = pd.DataFrame({"flow": flow, "gross": flow.abs() + 100.0}, index=fdays)
   124	    return rv, flow_df
   125	
   126	
   127	@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
   128	def world():
   129	    return _make_world()

   135	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN", flow_lag=1)
   136	    assert_no_lookahead(p)
   137	    return p

   242	def test_holiday_zeros_would_have_inflated_every_shock():
   243	    """Show the damage rather than asserting the rows exist. Fake zeros shrink the
   244	    20-day rolling sd, which INFLATES every |z| that follows -- that is the
   245	    channel through which C3 moved the test statistics."""
   246	    rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
   247	    fdays = us_equity_sessions(pd.Timestamp("2024-01-02"), pd.Timestamp("2024-12-31"))
   248	    clean = pd.Series(rng.normal(0, 300, len(fdays)), index=fdays)
   249	    holidays = pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-15", "2024-02-19", "2024-03-29"])
   250	    polluted = pd.concat([clean, pd.Series(0.0, index=holidays)]).sort_index()

   258	def test_deleting_a_sunday_is_rejected(world):
   259	    """Codex's test. `.shift(1)` moves by ROW POSITION, not by a day.
   260	
   261	    Punch one Sunday out and every later row silently becomes a t+2 forecast
   262	    wearing a t+1 label. v1's `assert_no_lookahead` re-derived its gap from the
   263	    very src_date the shift produced, so it could be fooled. The precondition
   264	    check cannot be.
   265	    """
   266	    rv, flow = world
   267	    sunday = next(d for d in rv.index if d.dayofweek == 6 and d > rv.index[100])
   268	    holed = rv.drop(index=sunday)
   269	
   270	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="hole"):
   271	        build_panel(holed, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   272

   275	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="duplicate"):
   276	        assert_calendar_is_complete(
   277	            pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-01", "2024-01-02", "2024-01-02"]), "dup"
   278	        )
   279	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="sorted"):
   280	        assert_calendar_is_complete(
   281	            pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-03", "2024-01-01", "2024-01-02"]), "unsorted"
   282	        )

   285	def test_future_data_cannot_move_a_past_forecast(world):
   286	    """Codex's test, and the strongest lookahead check there is: mutate the future
   287	    and demand that nothing already computed moves.
   288	
   289	    A src_date assertion can be satisfied by a leak that never touches src_date --
   290	    Codex demonstrated this by copying `y` straight into `z` and watching v1's
   291	    assertion still pass. This test cannot be fooled that way, because it never
   292	    asks the code where the data came from. It asks whether the answer changed.
   293	    """
   294	    rv, flow = world
   295	    p0 = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   296	    base = paired_oos(p0, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   297	    assert len(base.actual) > GW_MIN_LOSSES
   298	
   299	    cut = base.origins[len(base.origins) // 2]
   300	
   301	    rv_mut = rv.copy()
   302	    rv_mut.loc[rv_mut.index >= cut, "rv_gk"] *= 100.0      # blow up the future
   303	    flow_mut = flow.copy()
   304	    flow_mut.loc[flow_mut.index >= cut, "flow"] *= -50.0
   305	
   306	    p1 = build_panel(rv_mut, make_flow_features(flow_mut), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   307	    mut = paired_oos(p1, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   308	
   309	    keep0, keep1 = base.origins < cut, mut.origins < cut
   310	    assert keep0.sum() > 50
   311	    assert (base.origins[keep0] == mut.origins[keep1]).all()
   312	
   313	    np.testing.assert_allclose(base.pred_base[keep0], mut.pred_base[keep1], atol=1e-12)
   314	    np.testing.assert_allclose(base.pred_aug[keep0], mut.pred_aug[keep1], atol=1e-12)
   315	
   316	
   317	def test_a_rows_own_target_cannot_touch_its_own_forecast(world):
   318	    """Closes a hole the rev1 review found in the test above.
   319	
   320	    `test_future_data_cannot_move_a_past_forecast` only compares origins BEFORE the
   321	    mutation point, so it is blind to a SAME-ROW leak: set z_tau = y_tau and every
   322	    pre-cut forecast is still bit-identical, and the test waves it through. Codex
   323	    reproduced exactly that.
   324	
   325	    The invariant that does catch it: the forecast at origin i must not depend on
   326	    y[i]. Row i is never in its own training set (the embargo removes it), and the
   327	    design matrix must not contain the target. So perturb y at one row and demand
   328	    that the forecast AT THAT ORIGIN does not move.
   329	    """
   330	    rv, flow = world
   331	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   332	    clean = paired_oos(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   333	    j = len(clean.origins) // 2
   334	    origin = clean.origins[j]
   335	
   336	    bumped = p.df.copy()
   337	    bumped.loc[origin, "y"] *= 1000.0
   338	    out = paired_oos(
   339	        Panel(bumped, p.rv_col, p.horizon, p.asset, p.state_lag, p.flow_lag),
   340	        "HAR+ctrl",
   341	        "H1_absflow",
   342	    )
   343	    k = list(out.origins).index(origin)
   344	    assert out.pred_base[k] == pytest.approx(clean.pred_base[j], rel=1e-12)
   345	    assert out.pred_aug[k] == pytest.approx(clean.pred_aug[j], rel=1e-12)
   346	    # the ACTUAL must move -- otherwise the perturbation never landed and the test
   347	    # is vacuous
   348	    assert out.actual[k] != pytest.approx(clean.actual[j])
   349	
   350	
   351	def test_the_target_leak_codex_planted_is_actually_caught(world):
   352	    """The adversarial case, run for real: copy the target into a regressor.
   353	
   354	    v1's `assert_no_lookahead` passed under exactly this leak because it only ever
   355	    re-checked the src_date it had generated itself. The row-target invariant above
   356	    must fail loudly instead.
   357	    """
   358	    rv, flow = world
   359	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   360	    leaked = p.df.copy()
   361	    leaked["abs_z"] = np.log(leaked["y"])          # the leak: target -> regressor
   362	    lp = Panel(leaked, p.rv_col, p.horizon, p.asset, p.state_lag, p.flow_lag)
   363	
   364	    clean_out = paired_oos(lp, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   365	    j = len(clean_out.origins) // 2
   366	    origin = clean_out.origins[j]
   367	
   368	    bumped = leaked.copy()
   369	    bumped.loc[origin, "y"] *= 1000.0
   370	    bumped["abs_z"] = np.log(bumped["y"])
   371	    out = paired_oos(
   372	        Panel(bumped, p.rv_col, p.horizon, p.asset, p.state_lag, p.flow_lag),
   373	        "HAR+ctrl",
   374	        "H1_absflow",
   375	    )
   376	    k = list(out.origins).index(origin)
   377	
   378	    # baseline has no flow regressor, so it must be untouched...
   379	    assert out.pred_base[k] == pytest.approx(clean_out.pred_base[j], rel=1e-12)
   380	    # ...but the leaked model's own forecast MOVES with its own target. That is the
   381	    # signature of the leak, and the invariant test above is what detects it.
   382	    assert out.pred_aug[k] != pytest.approx(clean_out.pred_aug[j], rel=1e-9)
   383

   400	def test_unexpected_flow_ar5_lag_ordering():
   401	    """The AR design matrix is [lag1..lag5], so the prediction vector must be
   402	    f[i-1]..f[i-5]. Reversing it silently multiplies the lag-5 coefficient by the
   403	    lag-1 value. On a pure AR(1) the residual must collapse to ~0."""
   404	    n = 300
   405	    rng = np.random.default_rng(3)
   406	    f = np.zeros(n)
   407	    for i in range(1, n):
   408	        f[i] = 0.8 * f[i - 1] + rng.normal(0, 0.01)
   409	    s = pd.Series(f, index=pd.date_range("2024-01-01", periods=n))
   410	    assert ar_residual(s, p=5).dropna().abs().mean() < 0.05
   411

   545	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   546	def test_material_gain_exclusion_rejects_when_there_is_no_gain():
   547	    rng = np.random.default_rng(5)
   548	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.15, 400))
   549	    loss_aug = loss_base + rng.normal(0, 1e-4, 400)          # no gain
   550	    ex = material_gain_exclusion(loss_aug, loss_base, h=1, margin=0.01)
   551	    assert ex["z_stat"] > 0
   552	    assert ex["p_value_one_sided"] < 0.01
   553	
   554	
   555	def test_material_gain_exclusion_does_not_reject_when_the_gain_is_real():
   556	    """The other direction. Without this the exclusion test could be a rubber
   557	    stamp that always says "no material gain", and the bounded null would be
   558	    vacuous."""
   559	    rng = np.random.default_rng(6)
   560	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.15, 400))
   561	    ex = material_gain_exclusion(loss_base * 0.95, loss_base, h=1, margin=0.01)
   562	    assert ex["p_value_one_sided"] > 0.05                    # a real 5% gain
   563	
   564	
   565	def test_exclusion_margin_is_the_prespecified_one():
   566	    assert MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN == 0.01
   567	
   568	
   569	def test_inverted_upper_bound_brackets_the_true_gain():
   570	    """The bound is the ONLY object here that can legitimately bound the effect.
   571	
   572	    Plant a known 5% gain: the one-sided 95% upper bound must sit ABOVE it (a bound
   573	    that excluded the truth would be wrong) and must still be finite (a bound that
   574	    excluded nothing would be useless).
   575	    """
   576	    rng = np.random.default_rng(21)
   577	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.10, 800))
   578	    bound = qlike_gain_upper_bound(loss_base * 0.95, loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05)
   579	    assert bound is not None
   580	    assert bound > 5.0            # does not exclude the true 5% gain
   581	    assert bound < 20.0           # but still says something
   582	
   583	
   584	def test_inverted_upper_bound_is_tight_when_there_is_no_gain():
   585	    rng = np.random.default_rng(22)
   586	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.10, 800))
   587	    bound = qlike_gain_upper_bound(
   588	        loss_base + rng.normal(0, 1e-4, 800), loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05
   589	    )
   590	    assert bound is not None
   591	    assert bound < 3.0            # near-zero gain -> a tight bound
   592	
   593	
   594	def test_inverted_upper_bound_returns_none_when_nothing_can_be_excluded():
   595	    """A design that cannot exclude even a 90% gain must SAY so, not emit a number.
   596	
   597	    Here the augmented model really is ~95% better, so a 90% gain is genuinely NOT
   598	    excludable and the honest answer is "no bound". Emitting a number instead would
   599	    be the same class of error as v1's: manufacturing a bound the data cannot carry.
   600	    """
   601	    rng = np.random.default_rng(23)
   602	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.2, 300))
   603	    assert qlike_gain_upper_bound(loss_base * 0.05, loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05) is None
   604	
   605	
   606	def test_power_rises_with_the_effect_and_does_not_fire_on_noise():
   607	    """v1's "MDE" injected each beta ONCE into the single realised noise path and
   608	    took the first crossing: no repeated sampling, no size calibration, and a
   609	    curve that was not even monotone (its BTC CW fell from 1.713 at beta=.15 to
   610	    1.623 at beta=.8).
   611	
   612	    A real power curve must (a) not fire when the effect is zero and (b) rise with
   613	    the effect. Note (a) is asserted as an UPPER bound only: under GW's
   614	    method-level null a useless extra regressor makes the augmented method
   615	    genuinely worse, so the flow-favouring one-sided gate is conservative at
   616	    beta=0 by construction. A rate far BELOW 5% is correct behaviour; a rate above
   617	    it would be the alarm.
   618	    """
   619	    rv, flow = _make_world(n_days=800)
   620	    pw = power_simulation(
   621	        rv, make_flow_features(flow), "SYN", horizon=1, reps=60,
   622	        betas=(0.0, 0.30, 0.80),
   623	    )
   624	    curve = {r["beta"]: r["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] for r in pw["curve"]}
   625	    assert curve[0.0] <= 0.10                # never manufactures a flow signal
   626	    assert curve[0.80] > curve[0.0]
   627	    assert curve[0.80] > 0.50
   628	    assert pw["false_positive_rate_at_beta_0"] == curve[0.0]
   629	    assert "CONSERVATIVE at beta = 0" in pw["false_positive_note"]
   630

   640	    assert c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["z_stat"] < -1.645

   667	def test_holm_step_down_is_correct():
   668	    adj = holm([0.01, 0.02, 0.03])
   669	    assert adj[0] == pytest.approx(0.03)      # 3 * 0.01
   670	    assert adj == sorted(adj)                 # step-down enforces monotonicity
   671	    assert all(0.0 <= a <= 1.0 for a in adj)

   676	    verdict, so the raw values must survive all the way to serialization."""

   683	    """A real bug, pinned. The cell key joins the registry, the loss cache and the
   684	    verdict. When it was just `{asset}_h{h}_{alt}`, the primary BTC h=1 cell shared
   685	    a key with its rv-proxy, flow-lag, threshold and window variants -- so the dict

   709	
   710	def test_union_and_intersection_claims_get_opposite_multiplicity_treatment():
   711	    """The two families need OPPOSITE corrections, and the reason is logical, not
   712	    stylistic. Pinned because "be consistent, Holm everything" is the tempting
   713	    wrong move.
   714	
   715	    - "flow helps SOMEWHERE" is a UNION of alternatives: ten shots at finding an
   716	      effect, so the family-wise error rate must be controlled -> Holm.
   717	    - "flow helps NOWHERE by >= m" is an INTERSECTION: it may be asserted only if
   718	      EVERY cell rejects, which is an intersection-union test (Berger 1982) and
   719	      holds at level alpha with each cell UNADJUSTED. Holm there would inflate
   720	      type-II error and buy no type-I protection.
   721	
   722	    Concretely: a set of p-values that a conjunction claim should accept must be
   723	    accepted unadjusted, while the same p-values under a union claim must not all
   724	    survive Holm.
   725	    """
   726	    p = [0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.04]
   727	
   728	    # conjunction (exclusion): every cell rejects at an unadjusted 5%
   729	    assert all(x < 0.05 for x in p)
   730	
   731	    # union (GW): Holm must NOT wave all four through
   732	    assert not all(x < 0.05 for x in holm(p))
   733	
   734

   753	def test_normal_equations_match_lstsq(panel):
   754	    """The OOS loop solves the normal equations for speed (the power simulation
   755	    runs it ~16k times). It must be numerically identical to the SVD solution, or
   756	    the simulation and the headline would be using different estimators."""
   757	    d = panel.df.dropna(subset=SPECS["H1_absflow"])
   758	    X = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS["H1_absflow"]].to_numpy(float)])
   759	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   760	    for start in (0, 100, 200):
   761	        xtr = X[start : start + GW_TRAIN_WINDOW]
   762	        ytr = y[start : start + GW_TRAIN_WINDOW]
   763	        np.testing.assert_allclose(
   764	            np.linalg.solve(xtr.T @ xtr, xtr.T @ ytr),
   765	            np.linalg.lstsq(xtr, ytr, rcond=None)[0],
   766	            rtol=1e-7,
   767	            atol=1e-9,
   768	        )
   769	
   770	
   771	def test_ols_helper_agrees_with_the_oos_loop(panel):
   772	    d = panel.df.dropna(subset=SPECS["HAR+ctrl"])
   773	    X = d[SPECS["HAR+ctrl"]].to_numpy(float)
   774	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   775	    Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X])
   776	    np.testing.assert_allclose(
   777	        _ols(X, y), np.linalg.solve(Xc.T @ Xc, Xc.T @ y), rtol=1e-7, atol=1e-9
   778	    )
   779

   782	    """If none/own/shared all returned the same numbers, the smearing robustness
   783	    family would be theatre."""

   785	    none = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", smearing="none")
   786	    shared = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", smearing="shared")
   787	
   788	    assert not np.allclose(own.pred_aug, none.pred_aug)
   789	    assert not np.allclose(own.pred_aug, shared.pred_aug)
   790	    # 'shared' only overrides the AUGMENTED spec's multiplier
   791	    np.testing.assert_allclose(own.pred_base, shared.pred_base, rtol=1e-12)

   847	def test_power_bracket_reports_an_interval_not_a_solved_threshold():
   848	    """The grid is coarse, so the 80%-power effect can only be BRACKETED.
   849	
   850	    The rev1 re-review's R2: the previous revision published both a bracket and a
   851	    point (`rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct`), and the README then quoted the point.
   852	    A number that exists in the JSON will be quoted, so the point fields are gone,
   853	    and this test is the ratchet that keeps them gone.
   854	    """

   856	    pw = power_simulation(
   857	        rv, make_flow_features(flow), "SYN", horizon=1, reps=40,
   858	        betas=(0.0, 0.30, 0.90),
   859	    )

   864	        "beta_at_90pct_power", "rv_uplift_at_90pct_power_pct",
   865	    ):
   866	        assert banned not in pw, (
   867	            f"{banned!r} is a POINT estimate of a crossing that only a bracket can "
   868	            "locate. It was removed after the rev1 re-review; it must not come back."
   869	        )
   870	
   871	    br = pw["power_80pct_bracket"]
   872	    assert br is not None                       # always an object, never None
   873	    assert "reached_on_grid" in br
   874	    if br["reached_on_grid"]:
   875	        assert br["upper_grid_power"] >= 0.80
   876	        assert "bracket" in br["note"]
   877	        if br["lower_rv_uplift_pct"] is not None:
   878	            assert br["lower_grid_power"] < 0.80          # the target IS bracketed
   879	            assert br["lower_rv_uplift_pct"] < br["upper_rv_uplift_pct"]
   880	    else:
   881	        assert br["upper_rv_uplift_pct"] is None
   882	        assert "not reached" in br["note"]

   925	    """R4: the two raw-DM records are diagnostic for DIFFERENT reasons.

   927	    The expanding-window statistic is diagnostic because it is INVALID. The
   928	    fixed-window one is diagnostic because the verdict was pre-registered on the GW
   929	    object — it runs on the same GW-legal loss stream and agrees with the gate
   930	    statistic to ~3 decimals. Tagging the fixed-window statistic 'biased toward the
   931	    smaller model' imports the expanding-window pathology into the very design that
   932	    was changed to avoid it, and directly contradicts the file's own coincidence
   933	    note.
   934	    """
   935	    rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
   936	    la = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.2, 300))
   937	    lb = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.2, 300))
   938	
   939	    fixed = raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h=1, scheme="fixed")
   940	    expanding = raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h=1, scheme="expanding")
   941	
   942	    assert fixed["t_stat"] == pytest.approx(expanding["t_stat"])   # same arithmetic
   943	    assert fixed["feeds_gate"] is False and expanding["feeds_gate"] is False
   944	
   945	    # the discriminating substring: the fixed-window role may only ever say it is
   946	    # NOT biased; only the expanding one may assert the bias.
   947	    assert "is NOT biased toward the smaller model" in fixed["role"]
   948	    assert "is biased toward the smaller model" not in fixed["role"]
   949	    assert fixed["valid_for_this_nested_comparison"] is True
   950	    assert "not by invalidity" in fixed["role"].lower()
   951	
   952	    assert "is biased toward the smaller model" in expanding["role"]
   953	    assert expanding["valid_for_this_nested_comparison"] is False
   954	
   955	    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
   956	        raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h=1, scheme="whatever")
   957	
   958	
   959	def test_unbounded_memory_cell_is_flagged_not_silently_pooled(panel):
   960	    """R6: the AR(5)-unexpected regressor is built on an EXPANDING window, so that
   961	    cell's forecasting METHOD is not bounded-memory — which is a condition GW puts
   962	    on the whole method, not on the final regression. It must be labelled, not
   963	    quietly counted among the bounded-memory tests (and not dropped either: removing
   964	    a test once its result is known is selection)."""
   965	    ok = evaluate_cell(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="primary")
   966	    bad = evaluate_cell(
   967	        panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="flow_transform",
   968	        variant="unexp", bounded_memory=False,
   969	    )
   970	    assert ok["bounded_memory"] is True
   971	    assert bad["bounded_memory"] is False
   972	
   973	    gw = [r for r in REGISTRY if r.inference == "giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window"]
   974	    assert {r.bounded_memory for r in gw} == {True, False}
   975	    # every test of the offending cell inherits the flag, not just the GW one
   976	    assert all(
   977	        not r.bounded_memory for r in REGISTRY if r.cell == bad["cell"]
   978	    )

   277	    )

   299	        )

   301	    A(
   302	        "**Why these p-values are unadjusted, while the Giacomini-White ones above "
   303	        "are Holm-corrected.** The two claims have opposite logical structure, and "
   304	        "the correction has to follow the claim, not the habit. *\"Flow helps "
   305	        "somewhere\"* is a **union** of alternatives — ten shots at finding an effect "
   306	        "— so the family-wise error rate must be controlled. *\"Flow helps nowhere by "
   307	        "≥1%\"* is an **intersection**: it may be asserted only if *every* cell "
   308	        "rejects its own exclusion null, which is an intersection-union test "
   309	        "(Berger 1982) and holds at level α with each cell tested unadjusted. Holm "
   310	        "there would inflate type-II error and buy no type-I protection. The Holm "
   311	        "column is reported anyway so the choice is auditable — and note it does not "
   312	        "change the verdict either way."
   313	    )
   314	    A("")

   317	        f"cells reject H₀ at the pre-specified {vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% "
   318	        "margin. That margin is the project standard carried over from K1701 — it "
   319	        "was fixed before the results were seen, not tuned until the null looked "
   320	        "good. Because most cells cannot reject it, **the bounded null is not "
   321	        "established** and the verdict is `INCONCLUSIVE`, not `NULL`."
   322	    )
   323	    A("")
   324	    A("### What CAN be bounded: the inverted confidence interval")
   325	    A("")
   326	    A(
   327	        "The last column above is the honest quantitative answer. It is the "
   328	        "one-sided 95% **upper confidence bound** on the relative QLIKE gain, "
   329	        "obtained by inverting the exclusion test: gains *larger* than the bound are "
   330	        "excluded by the data; gains *smaller* than it are not. Unlike a power curve, "
   331	        "this is an inference about the effect rather than a property of the design "
   332	        "under an assumed truth — which is exactly the distinction v1 collapsed."
   333	    )
   334	    A("")
   335	    fb = vb.get("qlike_gain_upper_bound_family_simultaneous_pct")
   336	    if fb is not None:
   337	        A(
   338	            f"Holding **simultaneously across all {vb['cells_in_primary_family']} "
   339	            f"cells** (Bonferroni): the relative QLIKE gain from adding ETF flow is "
   340	            f"**≤ {fb:.1f}%**. Anything larger is ruled out; anything smaller is not."
   341	        )
   342	    else:
   343	        A(
   344	            "Simultaneously across the family, **no bound can be stated at all** — "
   345	            "at least one cell cannot exclude even a 90% relative QLIKE gain. That is "
   346	            "how little this sample constrains the effect size, and it is the "
   347	            "clearest possible refutation of v1's confident \"≥16% is excluded\"."
   348	        )
   349	    A("")

   352	        "*forecast accuracy*. It is **not** a statement that the RV uplift per flow "
   353	        "shock is smaller than any particular percentage, and it is **not** a proof "
   354	        "of exact zero."
   355	    )

   360	    A("")
   361	    pw = r["power_simulation"]
   362	    A(
   363	        f"{pw['BTC']['reps_per_beta']} simulated OOS paths per point. The DGP is the "
   364	        "fitted calendar-day HAR law of motion with block-bootstrapped innovations, "
   365	        "the real flow shocks and the real returns retained, and the effect injected "
   366	        "**into the law of motion** so it propagates through the HAR lags — exactly "
   367	        "as a genuine effect would, and exactly as the baseline would partially "
   368	        "absorb it."
   369	    )

   376	        f"| {num(pw['ETH']['false_positive_rate_at_beta_0'], 3)} |"
   377	    )
   378	    for lbl, key in (
   379	        ("80% power crossing lies in", "power_80pct_bracket"),
   380	        ("90% power crossing lies in", "power_90pct_bracket"),
   381	    ):
   382	        row = [lbl]
   383	        for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
   384	            br = pw[a][key]
   385	            if not br["reached_on_grid"]:
   386	                row.append(
   387	                    f"never reached — not even at +{pw[a]['max_uplift_tested_pct']}% "

   389	                )

   391	                row.append(f"at or below +{br['upper_rv_uplift_pct']}% RV uplift")

   396	                    f"{br['upper_grid_power']:.2f})"
   397	                )
   398	        A(f"| {row[0]} | {row[1]} | {row[2]} |")
   399	    A("")
   400	    A(
   401	        "Those are **intervals, not thresholds**. β runs on a coarse 8-point grid, so "
   402	        "the effect size at which power crosses a target can only be bracketed — it "
   403	        "sits somewhere strictly inside the interval. The results JSON deliberately "
   404	        "publishes **no point estimate** of an 80%- or 90%-power effect: turning a "
   405	        "coarse curve into a precise-sounding number is exactly the move that got v1 "
   406	        "failed, and a smaller version of it is still that move."
